Securing Your Agents: A Hands-On Guide to Agent Anomaly Detection

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

As highlighted in our announcement post, we said that each new model generation lets agents do more on their own. However, the more an agent can reach, the more one bad decision can cost. You've probably seen a few of these in the news lately:

Because these events produced no system crashes or logging errors, the teams behind these agents had no way to know until the damage occurred.

Agent Anomaly Detection on the Gemini Platform watches for exactly these issues. It reads the traces and logs your agents already produce and flags sessions where an agent goes outside the job it was built for.

Before you point it at your production agents, you'll probably want to see it work in a test environment. In this hands-on guide, we’ll walk you step-by-step through setting up Agent Anomaly Detection and using it to capture your very first agent behavior anomaly. By the end of this post, you'll have:

To get started, open the companion notebook Detect anomalous agent behavior with Agent Anomaly Detection in a separate tab (available on Google Colab or GitHub). As you read through this guide, it will prompt you when to execute notebook cells or interact directly with the Google Cloud console.

If a step can be done in the UI console, this post shows that path, with screenshots. The notebook does the same thing through the Agent Anomaly Detection API, so by the end you'll also have the code to automate enablement and pull findings into your own tooling.

2. A place to run the notebook: Google Colab, Colab Enterprise, or a local Jupyter environment. The notebook installs the latest ADK for Python itself. Agent Anomaly Detection is compatible with agents running ADK 1.2+, though ADK 2.0+ is preferred for best results.

3. A Cloud Logging bucket and an Observability dataset in either US or EU multi-region. (Both resources need to be in the same multi-region)

4. In the notebook: run the Get started cells. They install the packages, authenticate you, take your project ID, and turn on the four APIs that Agent Anomaly Detection needs.

First, let’s create a Customer Support Agent called “anomaly-detection-refund-demo”. Our example agent is responsible for helping customers check their order history and request refunds. However, it has a deliberate flaw: the agent has access to an overprivileged fetch_records tool which can read any table, including a `payroll` table containing sensitive employee data. All three parts of the lethal trifecta are here: the agent can read private data, it takes input from people it can't vet, and it sends what it finds back in its reply. Tool permissions and guardrails should stop most of this, but they only block what someone thought to write down. Agent Anomaly Detection flags the sessions that get past them, usually within a few minutes.

The notebook has the full agent. The part that matters is the tool, which never checks the table name against an allowlist:

All data is canned and in memory, so nothing real is read or refunded. This tool is intended to be over-permissive to illustrate AAD functionality. Don't copy this tool into production.

The Step 1 cells deploy the agent to Agent Runtime. Two details in the deploy cell are what make the agent visible to Agent Anomaly Detection. First, it sets three environment variables on the deployment, which turn on telemetry and write prompt and response content to Cloud Logging, where detection looks for it:

Second, it sends the agent one test message right after deploying. Agent Anomaly Detection discovers agents through their telemetry, so an agent that has never served a request does not appear under Monitored agents.

The deployment takes about 5 to 10 minutes and prints the agent's resource name and the reply to the test message:

For more information on how to create and deploy an agent, take a look at these guides: Creating an Agent and Deploying an Agent.

In the console: The sample code we provide already enables logging and telemetry when deploying the agent. Verify this by navigating to the Agent Deployments page (Gemini Platform > Agents > Deployments) and clicking “Configure” next to the recently created anomaly-detection-refund-demo agent to open the Observability Service Configuration.

Great! You now have a working agent that is successfully configured and ready to be monitored by Agent Anomaly Detection.

Now let’s set up Agent Anomaly Detection to monitor your agent’s traffic.

In the console: navigate to the Agent Platform > Agents > Audit page and click “Enable”.

Choose your agent’s Logging Bucket and Observability Dataset, making sure they are in the same region (us-multi-region or eu-multi-region) enable “Automatically monitor new agents”, then click “Agree and continue” and Agent Anomaly Detection will be set up in your project.

If you'd rather do this in code, the first Step 2 cell in the notebook creates the same configuration through the API, using the default _Default bucket and _Trace dataset with automatic monitoring on.

Setup takes around 30 minutes. Once it’s done loading you’re ready to proceed to the next step! Because you enabled automatic monitoring and the agent already served a request in Step 1, setup picks it up right away. Check that it shows as Active under Settings > Monitored agents before you continue; sessions that start earlier aren't analyzed.

Now that the agent is being monitored, let's see Agent Anomaly Detection in action! The Step 3 cells send one normal conversation and one anomalous conversation, each in its own session. The normal conversation is a customer listing their orders and getting a refund for a broken lamp. The anomalous conversation simulates a user who finds the fetch_records flaw and uses it to pull payroll data:

Notice that the user brings almost nothing: a customer ID and one first name. Asking for a table that doesn't exist makes the agent reveal the existing tables, which is how they learn there is a payroll table within reach.

The notebook prints both transcripts with the agent's tool calls, and the session IDs you'll look up next.

Anomaly Detection will scan the sessions within 5 minutes. In the landing page, you should see that a couple sessions have been processed and one anomalous session detected.

Let’s take a look at the anomalous session in more detail. Click the session id to open the detail view or paste the anomalous session id into the search bar.

In our run, the top finding was Tool misuse at High severity, with 95% probability. Your detectors and severities may differ, because the models behind the analysis are non-deterministic. The recommended solution is to restrict the `fetch_records` tool to read from specific tables.

Click an invocation in the Session pane to see its analysis in Details. Each invocation is Medium on its own, but together they make the session High.

To stay informed without watching the console, connect Agent Anomaly Detection to your existing alerting workflows.

In Cloud Monitoring: Agent Anomaly Detection publishes two metrics: Detected events, which counts anomalies by detector and severity, and Monitored agents, which tracks your detection coverage. To view these metrics, click the arrow next to "View in Cloud Monitoring" on the Anomaly detection page and select a metric to open it in Metrics Explorer.

To be notified of new anomalies, go to Monitoring > Alerting, click "Create policy", and select the Detected events metric. Leave the filters empty so the alert covers every anomaly, including the demo's, then set the rolling window to 5 minutes with the delta function, and set the threshold to above 0. Once you know what's normal for your agents, add a severity filter (for example High or Critical) so you're only alerted on the findings that matter. A metric-absence policy on Monitored agents will also alert you if monitoring stops.

In Security Command Center: Each anomaly is also published as an AGENT_SESSION_ANOMALY finding in the Security Command Center if it is enabled for your organization. Click "View in Security Command Center" to triage these findings alongside your other threats, use "Set security marks" to tag and track them, and set up notifications for high-severity findings.

You’ve now seen Agent Anomaly Detection in action: it successfully flagged the session where our agent went off-script to read payroll records, while evaluating the benign session, and correctly not flagging an anomaly. The finding told you why it was flagged and how to fix the underlying issue, and alerts in Cloud Monitoring and Security Command Center ensure you are notified of future anomalies.

When you're ready to secure your own production workloads, follow this rollout plan:

To review the complete list of prerequisites or to request Private Preview access for your project, head over to the Agent Anomaly Detection documentation.

TPU Model Design Considerations and Optimization Guide

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Google designed TPUs from the ground up to be exceptionally efficient for machine learning workloads, delivering top-tier performance per total cost of ownership (TCO). Because TPUs trade general-purpose hardware scheduling for wide systolic arrays, dedicated SparseCore engines, and custom interconnect fabrics, adhering to specific architectural best practices is essential to achieve maximum performance. To help developers get the most out of TPUs, we are launching the open-source AI-Hypercomputer/tpu-codesign guide. This post highlights its key principles—spanning shape alignment, architectural choices like LatentMoE, and parallelism strategies—to accelerate LLMs, Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models, and other hardware-intensive workloads on TPUs.

Performance tuning starts with model definition. Architectural choices made before the first training step set permanent ceilings on MFU (Model FLOP Utilization), cost, and serving latency. This guide gives concrete, hardware-grounded design rules for teams building or adapting models for TPUs, organized into three groups:

How to use this guide. The shape rules apply to anyone designing a new model. If you are adapting an existing model rather than designing one, the Expressing Parallelism section applies without touching the checkpoint. The general rules hold for any large-matmul architecture (dense LLMs, multimodal, and world models), and the companion AI-Hypercomputer/tpu-codesign repository grows model- and workload-specific sections over time.

Eighth-generation TPUs. These principles carry forward to TPU 8t and TPU 8i and to the newly announced TPU 8tx and TPU 8i Max. The reasoning stays the same and the thresholds move with the hardware. TPU 8t and TPU 8i both add native FP4. For training, TPU 8t also brings more balanced Vector Processing Unit (VPU) scaling. For serving, TPU 8i adds the Boardfly topology, a Collectives Acceleration Engine (CAE), and 3x more on-chip SRAM. We note the effects inline in Principles 5–8, and follow-up posts will cover eighth-generation designs in depth.

Measured evidence. Alongside the published sources, notes marked Measured carry results from our own Ironwood profiling program: open 20–30B MoEs (a Qwen3-30B-derived LatentMoE variant; GPT-OSS-20B) plus dense controls, single slice (except for the 512-chip TPU v7x and 1,024-chip TPU v4 ablations in Principle 4), BF16, training shapes up to 32K sequence, all against tuned-systems baselines. Treat the trends and rankings as transferable, the exact percentages as config-specific.

TPUs are purpose-built custom ML ASICs designed from the ground up to deliver state-of-the-art performance per total cost of ownership (TCO). While their architectural choices—such as wide 256×256 systolic arrays, software-managed VMEM, dedicated SparseCore engines, and direct multi-dimensional ICI fabrics—enable exceptional efficiency, they inherently create specific structural constraints and programming patterns that must be carefully followed to unlock maximal performance. Reaching peak efficiency requires aligning model shapes, routing, and parallelism directly with these underlying hardware characteristics:

Make d_model, FFN and expert intermediate widths (d_ff), latent widths, attention projections, and padded vocabulary sizes (used as contracting or output dimensions) multiples of 256 to match the 256×256 MXU systolic array. Token/batch axes and counts such as heads, layers, and experts are exempt. The most frequently missed dimension is head_dim: at 64 or 128, flash-attention QK products leave the MXU ≥ 50% idle. Reshape to head_dim = 256 with proportionally fewer Q and KV heads, preserving total projection widths and logical KV cache bytes (iso-quality in our ablations).

Measured: Reshaping Qwen3-30B MoE attention from 32 × 128 to 16 × 256 (identical parameters and FLOPs) sped up end-to-end training, with gains widening at longer context:

These rules apply for non-quadratic attention as well, notably GDN ablations from Yang et al. 2025 also noted at par or better quality for head dimension 256 for their Gated DeltaNet Attention.

XLA compiles each program for exact tensor shapes, so every buffer has a fixed address and every loop a known trip count, and a new shape means a recompilation and a host stall. What about dynamic shapes? Dynamic workloads fit this model: the trick is to separate the shape of a buffer from the extent of the data inside it.

Instead of padding to the largest group or the longest sequence, we keep the buffers at fixed shapes (tokens sorted by expert and padded only at the tail, the KV cache as a fixed pool of pages) and describe the ragged structure with a few small integer arrays (group sizes and offsets; KV lengths, the page table, cumulative query lengths). Pallas copies these into the TensorCore's scalar memory before the grid starts (PrefetchScalarGridSpec), and the kernel reads them to choose the next tile and bound its loops, with a mask covering a tile that straddles a group boundary or a partly filled last page. The data dependence becomes an address and a trip count instead of a shape, so the compiled program is the same for every routing and every batch composition and the kernel runs at the unpadded FLOP count.

Standard EP dispatches every routed token at full d_model width, making EP + FSDP slower than plain FSDP in our Ironwood benchmarks. LatentMoE projects routed tokens from d_model down to latent width ℓ (d/ℓ ≈ 2–4×) before dispatch, executes experts in latent space, and projects back up after combine (introduced by Nemotron 3 and adopted by 962B MAI-Base-1 and 2.8T Kimi K3):

Measured: On a 30B / 128-expert MoE, an ℓ = 768 (2.67×) LatentMoE cut on-device permute time 2.8×, lifted end-to-end training throughput +14% tokens/s (322 vs 270 TFLOP/s/chip, or +19%, since the latent projections add ~5% FLOPs per token; tokens stay ℓ-wide past the down-projection, so on-device permute and combine shrink alongside EP all-to-all), and freed ~25 GB/device of activation HBM when remat checkpoints were anchored on the compressed latent. While current MoE recipes in tpu-recipes run FSDP (+ TP), LatentMoE + FP8 dispatch makes EP roofline-viable when per-device batch is too small to hide FSDP weight all-gathers. Under both FSDP and EP, avoid over-fragmenting experts: small tokens × K / N group sizes starve MXU tiles.

When candidate MoE layouts match on quality, pick the one with higher compute intensity at a similar active-parameter budget. A larger shared expert (wider d_shared) shifts work into dense, communication-free GEMMs that overlap EP dispatch/combine in training and amortize weight reads across the decode batch. Fewer, wider routed experts (halving N and K while doubling d_ff at fixed sparsity K/N) cut dispatch traffic in half and double per-expert GEMM fill. Optimal (N, K) grows with model size, and over-coarsening hurts routing diversity. Ablate expert granularity and the shared-to-routed split at your target scale rather than copying GPU co-designed models, since the TPU optimum is more shared-heavy.

Measured: On a 63B LatentMoE (ℓ = 768, K/N = 1/32, 8K context, 512 TPU v7x chips at EP = 128), halving (N, K) from (512, 16) to (256, 8) while doubling d_ff and the shared expert lifted training throughput +57% tok/s and held lower loss across 120.8B tokens:

Low-concurrency decode streams model weights every token (~300 concurrent tokens per weight shard are needed in dense FP8 to reach compute roofline). Combine two architectural levers with explicit concurrency sizing:

Norms, activations, masking, routing math and quantization scale applies run on the Vector Processing Unit (VPU), which is roughly two orders of magnitude below the MXU in throughput and executes in sequence with the matmuls unless XLA fuses it into an adjacent GEMM or we move it off the TensorCore. Therefore we drop reshapes and transposes that carry no quality, standardize on fusable RMSNorm and SwiGLU so the elementwise work folds into the neighbouring GEMM, and offload the MoE sort, gather and scatter to the SparseCore.

Note that fused VPU work is free only while its cycle count fits inside the MXU window it is fused into. A quick illustration of this concept is block-scaled fp8 GEMM.

With a scale per block of B elements along the contraction axis, the MXU emits one partial sum per output element per block and the VPU rescales each one (about three vector ops), so per output element the MXU does 2K flops and the VPU does 3·ceil(K/B) ops. The rescale hides when B ≥ 1.5 × r_mxu / r_vpu, which on v7x (fp8 peak 4,614 TFLOPS against a 19.2 TFLOPS vector peak, a ratio near 240) puts the threshold near 340. In simulation on a DeepSeek-V3 expert shape, block 512 runs at the per-tensor time, block 256 is VPU-bound at 1.2x and block 128 at 2.4x. Achieved MXU efficiency is lower than peak, which lowers the effective ratio and can make the smaller blocks workable; the way to decide is to measure the GEMM at each block instead of using peak ratio. This threshold is specific to Ironwood. TPU 8t is designed with specialized support for scaling so quantization for MXFP4 and MXFP8 formats has no overhead. Re-measure the block-size threshold there instead of reusing 340. The rule itself carries forward: keep vector work hidden inside a matmul window.

For workloads dominated by BF16-sensitive matmuls (most notably long-context quadratic attention where direct FP8 degrades accuracy), native single-pass BF16 leaves MXU capacity unused whenever hardware low-precision MXU throughput exceeds native BF16 throughput by more than the pass count. Use the multi-pass BF16 algorithm to execute BF16-accurate matmuls via multiple passes over the higher-throughput lower-precision MXU pipeline without losing numerical fidelity. This matters more as low-precision throughput pulls ahead of BF16. TPU 8t adds native FP4, which doubles MXU throughput. On Ironwood the FP8 to BF16 ratio is exactly 2:1, so two or more passes cannot beat native BF16.

Assign communication-heavy parallel axes to the fastest physical links and keep logical mesh factors aligned with torus dimensions:

Minimum-degree CP matched to kernel trade-offs: All-gather CP is simplest (though its all-gathers can sit exposed) when paired with zigzag load balancing (context_parallel_load_balance in MaxText) so causal masking does not idle tail ranks; Ring CP overlaps collectives but trades off SplashAttention chunk efficiency; Ulysses A2A CP avoids causal skew but exposes all-to-alls and caps at head count.

Start every project with the quality-safe foundation: 256-aligned dimensions (1), static shapes with dropless routing (2), one parallelism plan mapped to physical links (8), wall-clock ablations on a tuned stack, and published reference targets (both in Measuring what matters below). For MoEs, add LatentMoE (3) after an ablation to cut routing/dispatch traffic and decode weight reads. Then prioritize by bottleneck regime:

Google engineered TPUs from the ground up as custom ML ASICs to deliver top-tier performance per total cost of ownership (TCO). Unlocking that potential requires bridging the gap between hardware architecture and model design. By standardizing on 256-aligned shapes, leveraging LatentMoE, and mapping parallel axes directly to physical links, engineering teams can maximize TPU efficiency across both training and serving workloads.

A reference blueprint is only as good as the loop used to validate it. Always evaluate architectural changes in wall-clock time on a tuned software stack rather than paper FLOPs: in our Ironwood benchmarks, pure systems tuning (SparseCore offload, MXU-aligned tiles, and remat tuning) lifted an open 30B MoE from 12% to 23% MFU before any model change, and published Ironwood baselines show 256-aligned MoEs (DeepSeek-V3, Qwen3-235B at 608–630 BF16 TFLOP/s/chip) running at roughly 2× the throughput of unaligned GPT-OSS-120B (330 TFLOP/s/chip). For the 6-signal XProf (profiler=xplane) triage checklist, roofline and HBM budgeting rules, wall-clock ablation methodology, published Ironwood reference targets, and ongoing updates as new TPU generations and kernels land, explore the living diagnostics guide and companion patterns in the open-source AI-Hypercomputer/tpu-codesign repository.

Thermal camera vs optical gas imaging on a robot dog: what each can and cannot detect

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 17 • Originally published at yuanzhirobot.com • AI-assisted

Short answer: A standard thermal camera measures surface temperature and finds overheating equipment, but it cannot see most gas leaks. An optical gas imaging (OGI) camera is a filtered infrared camera tuned to the absorption band of specific gases, so it shows leaks as a visible plume; it is heavier, costlier and qualitative unless extra quantification is added.

By the engineering team at YUANZHI ROBOTICS. We design and build sensor payloads for robot dogs, UGVs and drones. Originally published on our site; AI-assisted draft, reviewed by our engineers.

A standard thermal camera and an optical gas imaging (OGI) camera both work in the infrared, but they answer different questions. A thermal camera tells you how hot a surface is, so it finds overheating joints, bearings and motors. An OGI camera is filtered to the narrow band where a particular gas absorbs infrared, so it makes a leak visible as a moving plume. A thermal camera will not reliably show a gas leak, and an OGI camera is not the right tool for measuring equipment temperature. This guide explains the difference and how we decide what goes on a robot dog.

Thermal view separating a warm target from a cooler background: thermal cameras image surface temperature, not gas

Most thermal cameras used on robots are uncooled microbolometers working across the long-wave band, roughly 8 to 14 micrometres. They integrate energy over that whole band and convert it to a temperature for each pixel. That is exactly what you want for electrical and mechanical inspection: a loose busbar joint, a failing bearing or a blocked cooler shows up as a temperature difference against its neighbours, and a radiometric core lets the software store a number for each inspection point and compare it over time.

Most gases are almost transparent across that wide band, or absorb only in a narrow slice of it. Averaged over the whole band, a thin plume barely changes the reading, so on a normal thermal image the leak is invisible. Sometimes you see side effects, such as frost where expanding gas cools a fitting, but you cannot rely on that for leak detection.

An OGI camera puts a narrow band-pass filter in front of the detector, matched to an absorption band of the target gas. Gas in the field of view now absorbs or emits a noticeable share of the energy the camera sees, so it appears as a dark or light cloud against the background. Two families matter in practice:

One OGI camera does not see every gas. The camera is chosen for the gas list of the site, which is why we ask for that list before anything else.

Thermal options span a wide range on the same robot, and the choice is mostly about resolution, optics and whether you need pan-tilt. From the components we integrate:

An OGI camera has to be added on top of this budget. On a Unitree Go2 class robot that is rarely realistic; on a Unitree B2 or DEEP Robotics X30 it can be, after the weight and power budget is checked. Our payload weight budget guide shows how we do that.

Unitree B2 with a YUANZHI ROBOTICS sensor payload (3D render): heavier quadrupeds leave room for a gimbal and extra gas sensing

For substations and power plants the common set is a dual-spectrum thermal and zoom camera for equipment temperature and gauge reading, plus point sensors for the gas list, for example an NDIR SF6 module such as the Cubic SF6-015 (about 0.65 W) with electrochemical O2, CO and H2S cells. Point sensors give a concentration and an alarm threshold; they do not show where the leak is. Our gas detection payload guide covers sampling and calibration, and the substation payload guide shows the full set for that site type.

For oil, gas and chemical sites where locating hydrocarbon leaks is the main task, we add either a laser methane detector, which measures along a beam at a distance, or an OGI camera on a heavier robot, and keep the thermal camera for rotating equipment. Using point sensors to confirm what the image shows is more robust than relying on either alone.

Optical gas imaging view of a leak plume during a robot dog patrol: the gas itself becomes visible, but not its concentration

Send us your gas list, the equipment you need to measure and your robot model, and we will propose a thermal, gas and camera set that fits its payload limit. You can start in the payload configurator, look at the Unitree B2 or DEEP Robotics X30 platform pages, or read about our inspection robot and robot payload services.

Can a normal thermal camera on a robot dog detect gas leaks?

Generally no. A broadband 8-14 micrometre thermal camera shows surface temperature. It may hint at a leak indirectly, for example frost on a valve from expanding gas, but it does not image the gas itself. Seeing the gas needs an optical gas imaging camera filtered for that gas, or a point or laser gas sensor.

It depends on the filter. Cooled mid-wave OGI cameras are built for hydrocarbons such as methane, propane and VOCs. Long-wave OGI variants target gases such as SF6, ammonia and some refrigerants. One camera does not cover all gases, so the camera is chosen for the gas list of the site.

Does optical gas imaging tell you the concentration?

Not by itself. OGI shows where gas is and how the plume moves. Concentration or leak rate needs a quantification method with known distance, temperature contrast and wind, or a separate sensor such as a laser methane detector or a sampled point sensor.

Should I put both a thermal camera and gas sensors on one robot dog?

For most electrical and plant patrols, yes: a dual-spectrum thermal and zoom camera for equipment temperature, plus point gas sensors for the site gas list. Add an OGI camera only where finding hydrocarbon or SF6 leak sources visually is a core requirement, because it adds weight and cost.

More guides: Robot payload guides. Questions about a specific robot or site: lizhongpan@yuanzhirobot.com

Written by the engineering team at YUANZHI ROBOTICS. Original article: https://www.yuanzhirobot.com/en/guides/robot-dog-thermal-vs-optical-gas-imaging.html

GBase Database: What Do profile, onconfig, and sqlhosts Actually Do?

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

When initializing GBase Database(GBase 8s), three configuration files are easy to confuse: profile, onconfig, and sqlhosts.

All three may include the instance name as a suffix, but they serve completely different purposes.

Confusing them can send database troubleshooting in the wrong direction. Understanding their responsibilities is essential for database initialization, configuration management, and connection troubleshooting.

The easiest way to understand these files is to separate their responsibilities:

These files work together, but they are not interchangeable.

This file establishes the environment variables required by GBase Database.

This file defines instance-level configuration.

Production configurations contain additional parameters, including memory, buffer pool, and lock settings.

This file defines the connection entry for the instance.

For remote connections, the IP address, port, protocol, and corresponding listener configuration must agree.

The relationship can be understood as a simple configuration chain:

The profile establishes the environment, onconfig controls instance behavior, and sqlhosts provides connection information.

A mismatch in any of these layers can cause startup or connectivity problems.

The troubleshooting sequence should follow the responsibility of each file.

Check the instance name and configuration paths:

Then verify that the referenced files actually exist:

Check whether ROOTPATH points to the intended location and whether the required directory or device exists with the correct ownership and permissions.

Case B: The instance starts, but remote clients cannot connect

Then check whether the database listener is running and whether the port is listening:

Also verify firewall rules and network reachability.

The key is to avoid changing instance parameters when the actual problem is a connection configuration mismatch.

A quick consistency check can help identify mismatched instance names across the three files.

The expected output should include entries corresponding to:

If one of these entries is missing or uses a different instance name, investigate that file first.

This check does not validate every configuration detail, but it quickly rules out a common class of instance-name inconsistencies.

One particularly easy mistake is an incorrect ONCONFIG value.

For example, the environment may contain:

But the actual configuration file may have been renamed:

The environment variable is set, but its value does not point to the intended configuration file.

This is why checking whether a variable exists is insufficient.

A valid environment variable does not guarantee a valid configuration file.

GBase Database initialization scripts can generate the required configuration files automatically. During manual initialization, administrators may need to create and maintain them themselves.

Regardless of how the files are generated, verify their consistency after every configuration change.

I recommend including all three files in the database delivery baseline or configuration management process.

A few dozen lines of configuration can determine whether an instance starts correctly and whether clients can connect.

Also, assign clear ownership for maintaining these files. When different administrators independently modify profile, onconfig, and sqlhosts, configuration drift becomes much harder to identify.

A single owner, a documented baseline, and one consistency check can prevent many recurring connection and startup incidents.

For GBase Database(GBase 8s), remember the distinction: profile defines the environment, onconfig defines the instance, and sqlhosts defines the connection. Get these three files right before troubleshooting anything more complicated.

Arquitectura proporcional: no construyas un e-commerce para resolver una mudanza

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 17 • Originally published at juliancardozo.github.io

Publicado originalmente en juliancardozo.github.io.

Casa Médanos resolvía una necesidad acotada en tiempo: publicar, compartir y mantener disponible un inventario de muebles y electrodomésticos antes de una mudanza. El criterio de arquitectura fue operativo: eliminar trabajo manual y costo de mantenimiento, no anticipar capacidades de una plataforma de e-commerce que el caso no requería.

La tentación técnica era conocida: cuentas, pagos, carrito, backend, inventario. Pero el problema tenía una fecha concreta. Cada semana que pasaba importaba más publicar el catálogo que diseñar una plataforma capaz de sobrevivir diez años.

Esa restricción ordenó la decisión. Casa Médanos se convirtió en un catálogo rápido y compartible: fotos, precios visibles, filtros por ambiente, buscador y un estado claro para lo vendido. Un único link reemplazó una cantidad absurda de mensajes repetidos.

Elegir un sitio estático en GitHub Pages no fue recortar funcionalidad por deporte. Fue sacar de escena servidores que monitorear, bases que respaldar y costos que no ayudaban a vender un sillón. Para quien llega desde una red social, además, una página que carga rápido resuelve más que una arquitectura sofisticada.

La complejidad quedó donde sí había trabajo real: actualizar disponibilidad, encontrar un artículo y mostrar que algo ya se vendió sin hacerlo desaparecer. La tecnología siguió al flujo, no al revés.

Las preguntas repetidas justificaban una automatización puntual. Un flujo de n8n, versionado junto al código, ayuda a responder sin inventar una aplicación de mensajería ni convertir una venta temporal en una operación permanente.

Desde entonces uso una prueba sencilla: si una pieza de infraestructura no reduce una tarea repetitiva, un riesgo real o un costo presente, todavía no pertenece al MVP. Puede llegar después. Hoy solo distrae.

Principio de arquitectura: la solución debe responder a la vida útil, el riesgo y el volumen del problema actual. Toda capacidad extra también agrega costo operativo.

Del problema observado al MVP: diseñar producto desde el trabajo real del usuario

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 17 • Originally published at juliancardozo.github.io

Publicado originalmente en juliancardozo.github.io.

EscalaImprov comenzó con una necesidad observable: transformar teoría musical en decisiones inmediatas durante una jam. El caso ilustra una práctica de producto aplicable a cualquier negocio: partir de una fricción concreta, validarla como patrón y diseñar flujos distintos para los trabajos que el usuario realmente necesita resolver.

Tener un problema propio no lo convierte automáticamente en una oportunidad de mercado. Lo que sí aporta es una ventaja inicial: uno conoce el momento exacto en que aparece la frustración, los atajos que intenta y el lenguaje con el que la gente lo explica.

En este caso, no hacía falta una enciclopedia de teoría musical. Hacía falta feedback inmediato mientras se practica y una guía breve cuando se toca. Esa diferencia entre “aprender sobre algo” y “necesitar hacer algo” terminó definiendo el producto.

La experiencia propia es el comienzo de la investigación, no su reemplazo. Después hay que preguntar quién más vive esa fricción, cuándo aparece, qué alternativa usa y qué precio paga por no resolverla. Ahí se separa una historia interesante de un problema que merece producto.

Para EscalaImprov, esa observación llevó a separar dos momentos que parecían iguales: Estudio, para entender escalas, acordes e intervalos; y Jam, para tocar con una guía corta, notas de reposo y un click. No era una decisión estética: eran dos trabajos distintos.

Construir algo propio no deja solo una aplicación publicada. Deja una forma de mirar el siguiente problema. En este caso, diseñar una herramienta de estudio obligó a entender de verdad el círculo de quintas, el feedback instantáneo y por qué tocar con mouse es incómodo.

El mismo patrón se repite entre dominios muy distintos. MiCuota dejó una base de pagos auditables; Patio, decisiones de multi-tenancy; WebSpec AI, un flujo para especificar. El activo más interesante no siempre es el producto: a veces es la capacidad reutilizable que quedó detrás.

Principio de producto: una experiencia propia no reemplaza la validación, pero produce mejores hipótesis iniciales y ayuda a separar funcionalidades de trabajos reales del usuario.

Google’s Ask Maps Starts Finding Appointments & Calling Businesses via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

Webinar: 9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

Ask Maps is starting to find appointments online and call local businesses for customers. Here's what Business Profile says about Google's automated calls.

Open appointment slots at beauty, pet care, auto and home-repair businesses are starting to show up in Ask Maps, Google Maps’ AI chat. Google says Ask Maps can look for these appointments online and phone the businesses for a customer.

The feature is part of a set of Ask Maps updates that started rolling out Monday on Android and iOS. Appointment finding is launching first in the U.S.

Business Profile already has a setting for calls Google makes on behalf of customers. Google’s post doesn’t say whether Ask Maps calls follow it.

Google’s post describes appointment finding in Ask Maps this way:

“Now, with new agentic capabilities, you can choose to have Ask Maps find beauty, pet care, auto and home repair appointments online and call local businesses for you.”

Google’s example request is “Find me a 4 p.m. nail appointment near me.” Ask Maps then shows openings at nearby businesses “so it’s easy to decide what to book,” according to the post.

Google’s demo shows Ask Maps confirming the service, location and preferred time before checking availability.

Google’s Business Profile help page on automated calls says that when customers use Google Search or Maps to connect with a business, Google might call that business on their behalf to complete the request. The requests it lists include booking appointments and confirming the price and availability of services. If a business works with online booking providers, the page says Google may book through the partner or call the business directly.

Verified businesses manage these calls in Business Profile under More, then Business Profile settings, then Advanced settings. The section labeled “Google automated calls and text messages” has a toggle for “Bookings and inquiries from customers.” A business can also ask a Google caller to stop calling.

The help page says these calls are available in every U.S. state except Indiana, Louisiana, Minnesota, Montana and Nebraska.

Google has called local businesses for searchers before. In July 2025, I covered the rollout of AI calling in Search, where people searching for a service such as auto repair or a pet groomer can tap “Have AI check pricing” and have Google call several providers nearby. Google’s help page for that feature says the person then gets an email with quotes.

When it launched in Search, a Google spokesperson said the AI picks which businesses to contact based on traditional local search rankings. Google’s Ask Maps post doesn’t say how Ask Maps chooses businesses to check or call.

At I/O in May, Google said people could ask it to call businesses “for select categories like home repair, beauty or pet care.” Three of the four Ask Maps categories match those examples.

Google’s Ask Maps post says the chat is starting to expand to 30 countries across Europe, including Spain, Germany and the U.K., bringing it to more than 200 countries and territories. Itineraries and saved-list search are available everywhere Ask Maps is offered, while appointment finding starts in the U.S.

Ask Maps puts openings from nearby businesses in front of a customer in the same conversation where they pick a place to book. For beauty, pet care, auto and home-repair businesses in the U.S., that’s a new place to win an appointment, and Ask Maps can call them to check openings.

The “Bookings and inquiries from customers” toggle in Business Profile is the control Google documents for calls it makes for customers through Search and Maps. Check where yours is set, and let whoever answers your phone know these calls come from Google on a customer’s behalf.

Google says more regions are coming soon for Ask Maps’ appointment finding, though the post names none and gives no date. Watch the Business Profile help page for word on whether the “Bookings and inquiries from customers” toggle and the five-state exclusion cover Ask Maps’ calls.

Your Product Page Promises 7 Days. AliExpress Standard Takes 15 to 45.

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Your product page says delivery in 7 to 10 days — because the template said it, a competitor said it, or it felt like the number that would convert. On day ten the first "where is my order?" message arrives. Tracking shows the parcel moving normally, just not inside the window you promised. The customer is not wrong: the shipping method you used was never going to meet it.

AliExpress Standard Shipping typically takes 15 to 45 days depending on destination, season, and customs. That is not a supplier failure. It is the published reality of the cheapest default lane. Stores get into trouble not because shipping is slow, but because the promise on the page and the lane on the order describe two different businesses.

This post is about closing that gap: which AliExpress shipping lanes actually take how long, how to set a delivery window you can keep, and what to do when the honest window looks too slow to sell.

Sellers often pick a shipping option by price alone at order time, without mapping it to the promise they made at sale time. Here is the working comparison I use when auditing a store's setup, based on current carrier and seller guidance — always confirm against the estimated delivery date shown on the specific product, because supplier processing speed varies:

Estimates often hide processing and customs. Real delivery time is processing (often 1 to 3 days) plus transit plus customs (often 1 to 5 days): a "15-day transit" lane is an 18 to 23 day customer experience. Peak periods stretch everything — Black Friday, December holidays, and Chinese New Year in late January and February, when logistics pauses for one to two weeks or more. US sellers must also update landed-cost math: in May 2025 the US removed the $800 duty-free threshold for imports from China and Hong Kong, so parcels now carry additional duty cost by declared value.

Nobody sets out to mislead buyers. The short window creeps in: themes and import tools ship optimistic defaults nobody edits; sellers copy a competitor who may be making the same mistake; and there is a real fear that an honest window will kill conversion, so support absorbs the difference instead. But most buyers expect delivery within about 7 to 10 business days, so a Standard lane was never going to feel "normal" anyway. Hiding the wait does not shorten it — it only decides whether the customer feels informed or deceived. Sellers who state real times upfront consistently report fewer refund requests, because a buyer who knowingly accepted 18 days rarely disputes on day ten.

Step 1: Fix the lane per product before you write any text. For each product you actually advertise, decide which shipping option you will use at fulfilment — not the cheapest option on the listing, the one you will really pick. If you would never ship an advertised product by Super Economy, remove it from your mental math entirely.

Step 2: Build the window from the full formula. Processing plus realistic transit for your main destination plus a customs buffer — then publish the upper half of that range, not the best case. A plain sentence like "Delivery typically takes 15 to 25 days depending on your location," linked to a shipping page with the per-method table, beats a short number that is wrong. Specific and slightly conservative wins.

Step 3: Put the window where buyers decide. On the product page near the buy button, in checkout if your platform allows it, and in the confirmation email — with the same numbers everywhere. A mismatch between product page and policy page reads as a trap, because it is one.

Step 4: Make tracking reassure. Half of "where is my order" tickets are really "is anything happening?" tickets. Get the tracking number to your store and customer at ship time, and say when to expect first movement. A parcel visibly moving on day three generates a fraction of the tickets of a silent one. If tracking is copied by hand, that step is where this breaks — automate or checklist it before spending on ads.

Step 5: Pre-write delay messages and re-audit on change. Write a proactive delay note, a mid-window reassurance, and a missed-window apology with a remedy — before peak season. And when you switch suppliers, reset the window: a supplier change is a product-page change.

You sell a home accessory for $34.99 with free shipping, fulfilled by AliExpress Standard. Your page promises 7 to 10 days. Reality is 2 days processing plus 15 to 35 days transit plus customs — call it 19 to 40 days door to door. In a typical month you get 100 orders and roughly 25 "where is my order" contacts, 6 refund requests citing delivery time, and 2 chargebacks.

You have three honest options. One: keep Standard, publish "18 to 30 days," and accept a conversion dip. Two: switch to Premium at about $3.50 more per order, publish "8 to 15 days," and raise the price to $37.99 or absorb part of the cost — your break-even ad math tells you how much room you have. Three: source from a supplier with US warehouse stock at higher product cost and publish "3 to 7 days." There is no option four where Standard delivers in a week. Even halving delivery-related refunds just by publishing an honest window — no lane change — is worth more than the conversion it costs, because remaining buyers accepted the wait before paying.

Read your bestseller's page and find the delivery promise. Then open its last real order and check the lane and actual time. If those two numbers disagree, fix the page today — from the lane backwards, processing and customs included — and make sure tracking reaches the customer without a manual step in between. A slower promise you keep builds the reviews and repeat orders that a fast promise you break never will.

How far apart are the window on your product page and the lane you actually ship with? That gap, in days, is the most useful number in this whole post. Tell me yours in the comments.

— Jinguo, maker of GetDropFlow (https://getdropflow.com)

Personal Investments • Re: [Asset allocation, tax efficiency dilemma] I need some advice cuz I just don't know how to resolve this.

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

So AI indicated that your tax bill is $12,000, so it looks like you are paying all your taxes out of RMD withholding like me. So my solution is this....

Put all of your ex-wife's inheritance into a separate IRA (that you own of course). 20% of $1.74M is $348K. You (and your sister) have all the stocks in the other 80% of your portfolio. The ex-wife's fixed income earnings are enough to easily pay your taxes. So take part of your RMD from that IRA, and let your ex-wife pay your taxes. That seems like a win to me!

If stocks go down, you might even take the whole RMD from there so she doesn't get more than the 20% of your portfolio. Another win?

If stocks gone up, you win, and you might have to replenish the ex-wife IRA to get it back to 20%, but who cares, the stocks went up!

I can't think of any other solution because an IRA is tax deferred. Uncle Sam is going to get it sooner or later.

This really is all about your own attitude about paying taxes you have deferred paying. Make you asset allocation such that you can sleep at night, and do the small things you can do to pay a little less (like QCDs, tax efficient placement, the 100% treasury MM, etc).
BTW, not all of my tax obligation to the IRS and state is met with the withholding from the RMD, most of the IRS in conjunction to withholding each month from my pension is and I try to get close to $0 owed but some years I owe a little and some years I get a little back. I never withhold anything for state and that does come out from the RMD and again I usually owe a small amount or have a small refund. Whew!

OK, you got me on this one! This sounds quite creative but I did not know I could take some money out of my current IRA and put those dollars into another IRA at Vanguard! I read your post a few times and was having trouble understanding what that accomplishes. If one IRA is all equity and the other is all fixed income, haven't we just moved money from 1 pocket to another pocket but it's all the same dollar amount and the same AA? But aside from that, my equity AA is what I want to control in the taxable account when that big chunk of money goes to the settlement fund. Maybe the TMMF is the best option or treasuries as at least that avoids state tax. Ya know the accumulation phase was simple, this part of the game is complicated!

Prediction: Nov. 18 Will Be a Huge Day for AI Power Stocks. Here’s Why.

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia’s earnings, the first since Jensen Huang’s exit, could show whether AI spending is still climbing.

Nvidia (NVDA -4.12%) is coming off a huge week, and for all the wrong reasons. Founder Jensen Huang was forced to resign as CEO after the company's board began probing allegations that he had a relationship with a Chinese businesswoman who U.S. intelligence officials suspect was spying for Beijing. Congress is investigating, too.

Now Nvidia steps back on the world stage for its fiscal 2027 third-quarter earnings report, which is scheduled after the market closes Nov. 17. It will be led by interim CEO Colette Kress, who at least is a familiar figure in earnings calls -- she has been Nvidia's chief financial officer for more than a decade.

While Kress and other Nvidia executives will surely be peppered with questions about Huang and the company's investigation, there's still a lot of regular business to address. The quarterly reports are a window for investors to see how much money Nvidia is making, whether sales are accelerating or slowing, and to hear the company's short-term and long-term guidance.

Nvidia's reports can move markets because the company is central to the AI space, thanks to its leadership in making the most popular AI accelerators on the market. If Kress and her team at Nvidia demonstrate that demand for AI is continuing -- and AI spending remains at high levels -- then plenty of companies along the AI food chain could have really good days when the markets reopen on Nov. 18.

That's why investors in AI power stocks should be paying close attention, because companies supplying electricity and natural gas to AI data centers have been tremendous beneficiaries of investments in AI infrastructure.

AI is big business for Nvidia, and its growth numbers have been extraordinary.

Revenue for the second quarter was $96.2 billion, up 106% from a year ago. Nearly all that money comes from the company's powerful data center segment, which had revenue of $89 billion, up 117% from last year.

Nvidia has projected Q3 revenue of $108 billion, an 89% gain over the $57.1 billion it brought in a year ago. But revenue alone won't tell the entire story.

Investors will want to know whether Nvidia's customers are continuing to expand their AI infrastructure plans and whether the demand for the next-generation Vera Rubin platform is progressing as expected. Management's outlook for the fourth quarter could be particularly important in determining whether demand is accelerating or beginning to moderate.

If Nvidia demonstrates that its customers still have the financial resources and appetite to keep building AI data centers at their current pace, AI power companies could have very good weeks.

Constellation Energy (CEG -0.70%) is seeing more demand from big tech companies building the infrastructure to power AI. In September, it announced a 20-year, 690-megawatt power purchase agreement with Amazon to support its Calvert Cliffs nuclear facility. It already has a 20-year deal with Alphabet for about 890 megawatts of additional nuclear generating capacity.

Bloom Energy (BE -7.66%) makes solid oxide fuel cells that generate electricity on-site, and potentially allow data center operators to obtain power without waiting years for new transmission infrastructure. The company's Q3 revenue was $1.24 billion, up 138% from a year ago, and management raised its full-year revenue guidance to a range of $4.4 billion to $4.6 billion.

Energy Transfer's (ET -1.34%) natural gas pipeline network helps deliver the fuel used by gas-fired power plants, allowing them to provide reliable electricity for data centers. It recently announced agreements to transport natural gas to power plants under construction in Missouri. Growing electricity demand could create additional opportunities for its pipeline network as utilities and developers build more natural gas generation.

Nvidia's upcoming earnings report provides an important indication of how aggressively companies are investing in the technology -- even as some AI stocks have pulled back in recent weeks, and even as Nvidia goes through a crisis that nobody could have anticipated a year ago.

If Nvidia delivers strong results and an encouraging outlook, investors could become more confident that the data center expansion supporting Constellation Energy, Bloom Energy, and Energy Transfer has plenty of room to run.

De concurrentiemaatstaf voor AIDC verschuift van "rekenkracht" naar "rekenefficiÃ«ntie". De langetermijnontwikkeling van de rekenkracht van AI is in toenemende mate afhankelijk van de maximale energievoorziening en de efficiÃ«ntie van het hele conversieproces van energie naar tokens. Dit is precies de kernvraag die ZTE wil beantwoorden.

Hoogspanningsgelijkstroomsysteem van 800V: Het aanpakken van energieverlies bij de bron

Het eerste antwoord van ZTE is het hoogspanningsgelijkstroomsysteem van 800V, een fundamentele innovatie in de traditionele architectuur van stroomvoorzieningen. Met een efficiÃ«nt topologieontwerp, intelligent DC/DC-batterijbeheer, diverse toepassingsmogelijkheden en uitstekende schaalbaarheid levert het een compacte, zeer efficiÃ«nte, milieuvriendelijke en betrouwbare DC-voeding, waarmee de kernuitdagingen op gebied van stroomvoorziening voor computerinfrastructuren met een hoge dichtheid worden opgelost.

Intelligent beheer heeft ook een grote sprong vooruit gedaan: Het biedt een totaaloplossing voor het beheer van alle schakels en zichtbaarheid en controle van het volledige systeem, met functies zoals foutregistratie, temperatuurmeting op belangrijke locaties en selectieve beveiligingssimulatie. Voorspellend onderhoud wordt op drie niveaus uitgevoerd: modulefouten, systeemfouten en de levensduur van de batterij. Dit maakt beheer en onderhoud zorgelozer.

Dankzij de compatibiliteit met twee standaarden en het modulaire, flexibele ontwerp kan het systeem makkelijk schakelen tussen spanningsstandaarden (Â±400V/800V), waardoor een soepele overgang tussen oude en nieuwe scenario's mogelijk is. De capaciteit kan naar behoefte worden uitgebreid, waardoor druk op de initiÃ«le investering afneemt. Het dekt volledig gecentraliseerde grootschalige computerbases en gedistribueerde edge computing-upgradescenario's, en voldoet aan de behoeften van alle soorten klanten, waaronder operators, internetbedrijven en professionele investeerders in rekenkracht.

CDU voor vloeistofkoeling op megawattniveau: Innovatie doorbreekt het "thermische"  plafond

Hoewel efficiÃ«ntie aan de kant van de stroomvoorziening aanzienlijk verbetert, vereist ook de koeling een herziening van de capaciteit. De CDU heeft een koelcapaciteit van 1,5 MW per eenheid, uitbreidbaar tot 2,4 MW, met een flexibele configuratie van 2 tot 5 CDU-kasten. Naarmate de activiteit toeneemt kunnen hulpmodules naar behoefte worden geselecteerd en geÃ¼pgraded, waardoor de initiÃ«le investering en latere verspilling door aanpassingen aanzienlijk worden verminderd.

Dankzij de integratie van meerdere sensoren voor debiet, druk, temperatuur en meer, bereikt de CDU voor vloeistofkoeling van 1500 kW een nauwkeurige temperatuurregeling van Â±0,5 Â°C en een drukregeling van Â±0,05 bar, waardoor een stabiele werking van apparatuur met een hoge dichtheid wordt gegarandeerd. Het is uitgerust met gezondheidsindicatoren en statusweergave voor intelligent beheer en onderhoud, en beschikt over meerdere veiligheidsvoorzieningen zoals N+X redundante noodstroomvoorziening en dubbele stroomtoevoer, waardoor 24/7 ononderbroken werking mogelijk is.

De combinatie van hoogspanningsgelijkstroom van 800V en de CDU voor vloeistofkoeling van 1500 kW zorgt voor een efficiÃ«nt "inkomend" en "uitgaand" kanaal voor AIDC: De stroomvoorzieningszijde levert elke kilowattuur aan de server met minimaal verlies, terwijl de koelzijde de warmte met maximale efficiÃ«ntie afvoert, waardoor de PUE stabiel binnen de 1,15 blijft.

De toepassing in de praktijk is de enige maatstaf voor het valideren van technologische doeltreffendheid. De Nanjing Binjiang Data Center was een van de eersten die demonstratieresultaten presenteerde van de upgrade en transformatie: Het implementeerde een geÃ¯ntegreerde vloeistofkoelingsoplossing met een vermogensdichtheid tot 60 kW, waarbij gebruik werd gemaakt van zeer efficiÃ«nte HVDC-stroomdistributie en busbarsystemen, luchttoevoer van 32Â°C en groepsbesturingsoptimalisatie, waardoor een PUE van slechts 1,15 werd behaald. De ultieme "symfonie"  van watt, warmte en bit

In traditionele datacenters worden rekenkracht, stroomvoorziening, koeling en beheer en onderhoud doorgaans afzonderlijk gepland en gebouwd. Hackers inject credit card, crypto-stealing malware into 178 major websites

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Researchers say attackers compromised scripts delivered through Optimizely, exposing visitors to malware designed to replace crypto wallet addresses and steal payment card details.

Major global brands including Ledger, Salesforce and BBC served cryptocurrency and credit card-stealing malware to website visitors on Monday, in a major attack exploiting website optimization platform Optimizely, according to security researchers.

Dubbed “Optimjack” by researcher Amirul Iman, the campaign delivered malicious code through scripts hosted by Optimizely. The malware can replace legitimate cryptocurrency wallet addresses with attacker-controlled alternatives when visitors copy addresses. It also captured credit card details entered into website forms, according to a separate analysis by cybersecurity firm StepSecurity.

The campaign affected website scripts belonging to 178 organizations, including hardware wallet Ledger, OANDA / Coinpass and trading platforms IG, Interactive Investor and TMS Brokers, according to Report URI’s investigation. Other affected brands included IKEA, Salesforce, Zoom, the BBC and CNN, websites that collectively attract millions of visitors a day.

BBC was among the first websites observed to serve the Optimizely injection at around 4:21 am UTC on Monday. By 9:52 pm UTC, most of the malicious code had been removed, with Ledger’s snippet cleaned at about 8:41 pm UTC.

Cointelegraph reached out to Ledger, Coinpass, and a number of other major businesses reported to be impacted by the malicious injection.

This is a developing story, more information will be added as it becomes available.

Microdrama Writers At DramaWave Unionize With WGA West

Writers at one of the top microdrama platforms have organized with the Writers Guild of America West.

Following a successful card check, the guild revealed Monday that the writers at DramaWave have organized a union to bargain over workplace issues such as compensation and benefits, job security, a fair crediting system, and guardrails on the use of AI in writing.

“We love writing for DramaWave’s rapidly growing audience, and we’re excited to organize with the WGA for a voice on the job and the protections and benefits a union contract can provide,” Senior Writer and DramaWave Organizing Committee member Peter Forbes said in a statement. “DramaWave did the right thing by quickly recognizing the union and we look forward to negotiating a fair contract.” Related Stories News Will The Congressional Blue Tsunami Help Or Hurt The Plan To Save Hollywood? News Hollywood Unions Wonder Whether Next California Governor Will Share Newsom's Commitment To Expanding Film Tax Credits

Per the WGAW, DramaWave currently employs 27 writers, who began their organizing effort over the summer. They delivered The writers delivered a letter to DramaWave demanding recognition of the WGA as their collective bargaining representative on October 28. The company agreed to give the union voluntary recognition pending the card check. Watch on Deadline

Bargaining dates for the first contract have not yet been determined.

Adds WGAW President Michele Mulroney: “DramaWave is a top microdrama app because its writers tell stories that captivate audiences worldwide. We are proud to welcome them into our union. Microdramas are a growing part of the entertainment industry and it’s critical that these writing jobs be union jobs.”

Best Isekai Books Like Dungeon Crawler Carl

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Dungeon Crawler Carl by Matt Dinniman is not, technically, an isekai. The genre was popularized in Japan, and in the genre's current form, it centers around an ordinary person who's suddenly transported or reborn into another world. Carl isn't relocated to a different world—the madness comes to his world. But it does have all the other makings of an isekai: an ordinary dude has to go up against RPG-like elements such as monsters, leveling up, loot, questing, and so on.

Of course, there are plenty of litRPG books for fans of DCC, but there are also plenty of isekai that fans of Dinniman's books would enjoy. Lots of manga and anime play off the "adventuring in another world" trope, but we'll take a look at the light novels, specifically, which are breezy, fast-paced and fun to read bite-sized novels that are designed to be portable.

Grab one (or all) of these recommendations, because you never know when you might need some light reading when you're out and about on your own epic adventures... or just commuting to work. Plus, quite a few of these have accompanying anime, so you can continue the journey off the page, too!

In So I'm A Spider, So What? (available for purchase here) a freak accident causes an entire Japanese high school to be obliterated and killed. Most are reincarnated into another world as nobles, adventurers, and other characters. But one girl is reincarnated in a dungeon as the lowest and weakest monster of them all: a tiny spider.

Of course, part of the fun of an isekai is watching the underdog go from nothing to being kind of a big deal in the world, just like in Dungeon Crawler Carl. Over her adventures, the spider grows more and more powerful, makes some very useful allies (and, of course, some dangerous enemies), and becomes an influential figure in this strange new world.

The protagonist of Completionist Chronicles (available for purchase here) is an ordinary Joe, literally. Unlike in a traditional isekai, Joe doesn't get run over by a truck or die to get reborn. Instead, he makes a choice to permanently start over his life inside a game.

As soon as he does, Joe realizes that he has some abilities that make him a wanted man in the world. Forced to practice his forbidden magic in secret, he makes it his mission to learn more about the world. Joe won't settle for just a slight glimpse of the game; he's after that 100% completion achievement. He's going to get every item, skill, ability, and every bit of knowledge that this world has to offer, or die trying.

The Rising Of The Shield Hero (available here) follows Naofumi, just an ordinary nerdy guy who's suddenly summoned to another universe. He learns that he's one of the four legendary heroes who are destined to save the world from destruction. Unfortunately, he's the weakest and least impressive of them all: the Shield Hero.

Without a powerful weapon, Naofumi has to rely on his brains, strategies, and loyal allies who he meets along the way. Together, they fight monsters, unravel political plots, and find a way to rise above his weak-seeming shield and do his part to save this fantasy world he's found himself in.

In Dungeon Crawler Carl, Carl's survival depends largely on his use of, essentially, a hidden tool. That's also the case for Solo Leveling (available for purchase here). This series is also technically not an isekai, since Jinwoo Sung isn't transported to another world permanently. Instead, mysterious Gates suddenly appear in his world, which connect to other worlds full of dangerous monsters.

In Jinwoo's world, some people "awaken" to become fighters while others stay as weak non-fighters. Jinwoo is one of the latter. The thing is, once you awaken, you're assigned a rank, and you stay at that rank forever. One day, Jinwoo finds that he can do something no one else can: he can level up.

This changes everything. As the only person in the world who can level up and increase his strength, Jinwoo grows in power by fighting through dungeons. As he does, he uncovers the secrets of these Gates and what's happening in his world.

The idea behind Log Horizon (available for purchase here) is based around an MMORPG—a massively multiplayer online role-playing game. One day, 30,000 players wake up to find themselves trapped inside their beloved multiplayer video game, Elder Tales. The game becomes much more serious when they realize that they might be stuck in this digital world forever, with no way to escape.

A group of adventurers bands together to find a way back to their home world. They travel around the fantasy world, defeat monsters, and search for clues about what's happening. And soon, they discover something even more alarming: They respawn after death, but lose a little more of their memories each time. The fight to survive and return home begins now!

With two such similar genres as litRPG and isekai, there's bound to be some overlap. He Who Fights with Monsters (available for purchase here) sits right at that intersection between the Western litRPG and Japanese isekai.

Jason is just your run-of-the-mill office supplies middle manager until he's transported to another world. On his very first day, he has to fend against monsters, wizards, cultists, and cannibals. Like Carl, Jason has to use his wits and some special powers to stay alive in this wild world. The only problem is that he's a pretty decent guy, but all his powers are evil.

In DCC, Carl's cat Princess Donut captures the spotlight. The young cat girl in Reincarnated as as Sword (available for purchase here) is also at the center of the story, but as the title suggests, she's not the main character here.

Instead, the protagonist is an office worker who's reincarnated in the fantast world as a sentient sword. He remembers his old life, but not his name, so he becomes the girl's Teacher in both name and status. They work together to achieve both their goals: She wants to become stronger, and he wants to know why he's in this world. They learn more about themselves, too, and become an unstoppable team.

In That Time I Got Reincarnated As A Slime (available for purchase here), a guy stuck at a dead-end job meets his untimely end at the hands of a robber. As he's dying, he unknowingly makes a pact with a strange system that resurrects him in another world... as a slime, of course.

Like the spider from So I'm a Spider, So What?, the little slime doesn't stay weak for very long. He uses his special connection with the aforementioned system to acquire some extremely useful skills, then heads out on his adventure.

As the slime meets new characters, he uses his strategic way of thinking to forge some extremely powerful alliances. Just like Dungeon Crawler Carl, the slime's story revolves around a fight for survival and acquiring power. And just like DCC, the slime gets more and more involved in the overall lore and politics of the world, sending him on more epic adventures each time.

SpaceX Just Brought a Starship Back From Orbit and Caught It. Here's the Part of the Business That Gains the Most.

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

With the ship caught but the booster lost, why could Starlink gain the most?

SpaceX (SPCX -1.26%) got half its rocket home. On Saturday, Nov. 14, about 22.5 hours after Starship took off from Starbase, Texas, the launch tower's arms closed around the rocket's upper stage (the part SpaceX calls the ship). It was the first time SpaceX has caught a ship.

The lower half didn't make it. A day earlier, the Super Heavy booster's guidance system canceled its catch and steered it out over the Gulf of Mexico, where its flight termination system destroyed it after a landing burn.

Before it came home, the ship delivered 26 of Starlink's newer V3 satellites. And I think Starlink is the part of SpaceX that gains the most from a ship it can catch. After all, it uses a big chunk of SpaceX's launch capacity, and it makes most of the company's operating profit.

The growth stock finished near $139 on Friday, Nov. 13, the last session before Saturday's landing, about 38% under its post-IPO high of $225.64.

SpaceX has caught and reflown Super Heavy boosters before. The ship was the half it had never caught.

On this flight, though, the booster's boostback burn (which turns it back toward the launch site) stopped early after a low-fuel alarm, according to SpaceX, which is still looking into the root cause. So SpaceX still hasn't caught both halves of one rocket on the same flight.

Last month, I argued that the stock wouldn't get full credit for Starship until SpaceX could reuse the whole rocket. Saturday's catch covers half of that. And no caught ship has flown a second time yet. But CEO Elon Musk said on Sunday that this one could probably fly again after a few weeks of refurbishment.

SpaceX records no revenue for launching its own Starlink satellites. Instead, the launch cost is added to the satellites' value on its balance sheet and expensed over time as depreciation in the connectivity segment.

And it launches its own payloads often: 21 of its 33 Falcon launches in the third quarter were internal missions, and every Starship launch so far has been too.

Showing what this build-out costs, connectivity depreciation and amortization climbed 57% year over year to $986 million in the third quarter. This was up 22% from the second quarter, the same rate as the segment's revenue. In other words, the cost of the network is growing as fast as the sales it supports.

Starlink's already very profitable, of course. Connectivity operating income almost doubled year over year to around $2.4 billion. The segment's operating margin kept growing too, hitting around 45%, up from 38% a year earlier and 39% in the second quarter. CFO Bret Johnsen said the quarter had a one-time benefit, mostly from tariff refunds, worth about 3 percentage points of the segment's gross margin. Even without it, the operating margin widened.

This makes connectivity around 36% of SpaceX's third-quarter revenue but about 85% of the operating income from its three segments. Notably, the superintelligence segment, SpaceX's artificial intelligence (AI) business, pulled in about half of all revenue but earned just $431 million, held down by about $1.2 billion in stock-based compensation linked to acquisitions.

In short, Starlink is the profit engine. A cheaper launch might cut the cost of every satellite added to its books, and the depreciation charged against that profit for years to come.

"I think we'll need a few hundred V3 satellites for people to really feel the impact," SpaceX President Gwynne Shotwell said on the company's third-quarter earnings call in October. She thinks SpaceX can get there by the middle of next year.

Starship has delivered 52 V3 satellites on its two orbital flights, 26 apiece, so every 100 more takes about four flights. If each flight used up a new ship, every load of satellites would carry the cost of a whole upper stage. A ship that's caught and flown again spreads that cost over many launches.

Friday's close puts SpaceX at around $1.9 trillion. Using annualized third-quarter revenue, the stock trades at around 32 times sales. At that valuation, I think investors are already paying for a large share of the savings a reusable Starship could deliver.

Saturday's catch got SpaceX closer to those savings. But they won't show in Starlink's depreciation until caught ships fly again, and the booster still needs to come home on the same flight as the ship. I'd want to see both before paying this much for the shares.

Louisiana blocks prediction markets and threatens $370M in revenue from exchanges

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Louisiana’s crackdown on prediction markets targets Robinhood and Coinbase intermediaries as the CFTC challenges state enforcement.

Louisiana's Nov. 12 order related to prediction markets reaches the brokers and clearing firms behind the apps, as well as the exchanges themselves.

Its scope, documented in the CFTC's federal complaint, exposes a shared legal risk for platforms earning income from different kinds of trading.

Robinhood and Coinbase each report prediction-market revenue alongside their crypto businesses. Louisiana's action reaches several layers of the trading chain, including the intermediary serving the customer.

According to the CFTC's complaint, Louisiana obtained an ex parte temporary restraining order on Nov. 12 against entities including registered exchanges, futures commission merchants and derivatives clearing organizations. Ex parte means the initial order was obtained without the other side being heard.

The complaint quotes restrictions on offering, clearing, settling, brokering and otherwise facilitating specified event contracts involving people located in Louisiana without the required state license and permit.

Its description includes sports, elections, and award events within the order's stated statutory scope.

The court-filed copy of the order names Robinhood Derivatives and Coinbase Financial Markets, along with multiple Kalshi entities, ForecastEx and Rothera.

Robinhood's product disclosure identifies Robinhood Derivatives as the intermediary and lists KalshiEX, ForecastEx, Rothera, and Crypto.com Derivatives North America as venues. Coinbase says it offers its contracts through Coinbase Financial Markets and has Kalshi operate them, which determines outcomes.

Robinhood already has several exchange routes. If the same restricted broker remains involved, moving a contract to another venue leaves the intermediary within the order's documented reach. That conditional legal-scope analysis leaves each company's implementation unconfirmed.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The Nov. 12 order reaches specified event-contract activities for Louisiana-located people where operators lack the required state license and permit.

Robinhood Derivatives offers contracts through several named exchange venues.

Coinbase Financial Markets offers contracts operated by Kalshi.

A change of exchange alone does not establish protection where the same restricted broker remains involved.

Louisiana scope only. This diagram does not establish a final jurisdiction ruling or the order's present effect beyond the reviewed records. Actual new-order, existing-position, settlement and withdrawal treatment remains unconfirmed. Named venues are those in the reviewed disclosures.

Map the regulated roles and covered activities described in the CFTC complaint. Select the two documented platform disclosures to show their named intermediary and exchange relationships. Lines represent legal reach or product roles, not cash flows. No quantitative comparison or calculation.

Louisiana scope only. This diagram does not establish a final jurisdiction ruling or the order's present effect beyond the reviewed records. Actual new-order, existing-position, settlement and withdrawal treatment remains unconfirmed. Named venues are those in the reviewed disclosures.

Robinhood reported $173 million in event-contract revenue for the quarter ended Sept. 30, more than six times the year-earlier figure. Cryptocurrency revenue was $120 million, down 55%.

Coinbase's Oct. 29 announcement said prediction markets had surpassed $200 million in annualized revenue.

The US and CFTC sued Louisiana and state officials on Nov. 13, arguing that federal derivatives law preempts the state's enforcement. They seek declaratory and injunctive relief, including a bar on enforcing or extending the state order.

The government's motion for a temporary restraining order and preliminary injunction is listed for submission Nov. 17. In Louisiana's enforcement case, which moved to federal court, Robinhood seeks to vacate the state order, and Rothera seeks its dissolution or modification.

In a separate federal case, Kalshi's Nov. 13 oral request concerned withdrawal of its own temporary-restraint application, with its preliminary-injunction hearing scheduled for Nov. 23.

Louisiana-specific treatment of new orders, existing positions, settlement and withdrawals remains unconfirmed.

Prediction markets can add income beyond crypto trading while leaving competing platforms exposed to the same state restrictions. A different exchange partner alone offers no demonstrated protection where the order also reaches the broker.
