Best Bitcoin Staking Platforms in 2026: BTC Rewards, Custody and Risks Compared

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

Bitcoin staking allows holders to earn rewards on their BTC without selling the underlying asset. In 2026, Stacks, Babylon, Core, Starknet and Stacking DAO offer different approaches, with varying rewards, custody models and staking requirements.

Stacks targets approximately 3% annualized rewards in BTC, while Babylon, Core and Starknet distribute rewards in their native tokens. Stacking DAO offers liquid staking with BTC-linked returns.

Choosing a platform requires looking beyond advertised yields to understand token requirements, custody arrangements and withdrawal conditions.

Rates as of November 10, 2026. Stacks quotes a target annual yield, Babylon quotes APR, and other displayed rates may use different calculation conventions.

The main differences concern reward sources, custody arrangements, additional capital requirements and withdrawal flexibility.

Bitcoin staking rewards vary by source, denomination and additional capital requirements.

Stacks is a Bitcoin layer where BTC holders earn BTC.

Stacks miners commit BTC through Proof of Transfer (PoX) to compete for the right to produce Stacks blocks and earn STX rewards. The committed BTC is distributed to staking participants, providing the source of their BTC rewards. PoX has distributed over 4,200 BTC since January 2021.

Direct Bitcoin staking targets approximately 3% annualized yield, with weekly BTC rewards. Since September 2026, three staking bonds have sold out, bringing total BTC staked to 1,740 BTC.

Babylon distributes BABY rewards funded by newly issued tokens through the protocol’s inflation mechanism. Its displayed APR ranges from 0.04% to 0.52%, with the higher rate requiring participants to co-stake BABY alongside BTC.

Returns depend on reward distribution and BABY’s value relative to Bitcoin.

Core distributes CORE rewards to participants who delegate BTC to validators securing its blockchain. Rewards come from newly issued CORE block rewards and transaction fees.

Its displayed top-tier yield is 0.79%, with higher reward tiers requiring participants to stake CORE alongside BTC.

Starknet allows holders of supported tokenized Bitcoin assets to delegate them to validators securing the network.

Participants earn newly minted STRK tokens, with a displayed yield of 2.91%. Actual returns depend on network participation and STRK’s value relative to BTC.

Stacking DAO provides liquid Bitcoin staking through stBTC, a token representing an sBTC-based staking position. BTC rewards generated through Bitcoin staking on Stacks accumulate in the position, increasing the amount of sBTC backing each stBTC over time.

Its displayed yield is 2.31%, with rewards accruing automatically rather than requiring separate claims.

A 4% annual reward paid in a token that loses 50% of its value against BTC would be worth approximately 2% of the original BTC position, assuming other factors remain unchanged.

BTC rewards avoid this specific reward-token conversion risk, although staking still involves other costs and risks.

Stacks, Babylon and Core support native Bitcoin staking on Bitcoin L1, while Starknet and Stacking DAO use Bitcoin-backed assets on other layers.

In direct self-custodial staking, BTC remains timelocked on Bitcoin L1 under the participant’s custody. Institutions can also access this route through qualified custody infrastructure, including BitGo and Anchorage Digital.

BTC is locked on Bitcoin L1 and delegated to finality providers supporting the Babylon ecosystem. Bitcoin remains native, but the staking position is subject to protocol slashing conditions.

BTC holders timelock their Bitcoin on L1 and delegate staking weight to Core validators without transferring BTC to another chain.

Participants stake supported tokenized Bitcoin assets on Starknet, introducing dependencies on the underlying token’s backing and smart contracts.

Participants can deposit sBTC, a Bitcoin-backed asset on Stacks, or BTC, which is converted into sBTC. In return, they receive stBTC, representing their staking position and accruing BTC rewards. Part of Stacks’ Bitcoin staking capacity is allocated to this pooled staking route. Unlike direct staking on Stacks, this route is not self-custodial.

Native Bitcoin custody avoids certain bridge and wrapper dependencies but does not remove staking-specific risks.

Some protocols require additional token commitments, while others use them to increase rewards.

Stacks uses STX as staking capacity. Direct participants commit STX worth approximately 5% of their BTC position, locked for the staking term.

Babylon offers increased rewards through BABY co-staking, while Core uses CORE dual staking to determine reward tiers.

Starknet does not require participants to pair STRK with their BTC for standard delegation. Stacking DAO handles the underlying STX commitment without requiring participants to provide it separately.

These requirements affect capital efficiency because additional tokens introduce further price exposure.

Withdrawal conditions vary across staking models.

Stacks uses approximately six-month bonding periods for direct staking. Participants can exit early and recover BTC, but forfeit remaining rewards, while paired STX stays locked until the original term ends.

Babylon supports unbonding, subject to protocol-defined waiting periods and transaction requirements.

Core allows participants to choose how long to timelock their BTC on Bitcoin L1, with a minimum staking period of 24 hours. Once the term expires, BTC can be redeemed through a Bitcoin transaction.

Starknet allows participants to unstake tokenized BTC, subject to a seven-day withdrawal period during which rewards stop accruing. Liquid staking providers may offer alternative exit routes.

Stacking DAO supports instant stBTC-to-sBTC withdrawals when sufficient liquidity is available, subject to fees. Alternatively, participants can use a standard withdrawal with an approximately four-week cooldown. Converting sBTC into native BTC requires a separate withdrawal process.

Liquid staking provides flexibility, but instant redemption depends on available liquidity and protocol conditions.

Bitcoin staking risks depend on custody, reward assets and protocol conditions.

Token exposure: Stacks requires STX for direct staking, while Babylon and Core offer higher rewards through BABY and CORE commitments. Token price changes affect overall returns.

Slashing: Babylon can penalize staked BTC when delegated finality providers violate specified security conditions. Stacks’ self-custodial path does not apply BTC slashing.

Smart contracts: Starknet and Stacking DAO rely on tokenized Bitcoin infrastructure, introducing smart contract and asset-backing risks.

Liquidity and lockups: Native staking can restrict withdrawals, while liquid staking positions depend on redemption mechanisms and market liquidity.

Quoted yields should therefore be evaluated alongside custody arrangements, token requirements and withdrawal conditions.

Stacks is a strong option for eligible holders prioritizing BTC rewards and native custody. Babylon and Core offer alternative native staking models, while Starknet and Stacking DAO provide tokenized Bitcoin staking options.

Among the quoted figures, Stacks has the highest stated rate at approximately 3% target annual yield, followed by Starknet at 2.91%. Reward denominations and calculation methods differ, so these rates are not directly comparable.

Yes. Stacks distributes BTC rewards through direct Bitcoin staking, while Stacking DAO provides BTC-linked rewards through stBTC. Babylon, Core and Starknet distribute rewards in their respective native tokens.

Yes. Stacks, Babylon and Core support native BTC staking on Bitcoin L1. Their mechanisms differ in token requirements, withdrawal conditions and slashing exposure.

Risks include reward-token volatility, additional capital requirements, slashing, lockups, smart contract vulnerabilities and liquidity constraints. The relevant risks depend on the platform and staking method.

Prediction: Nvidia Stock Will Beat the Market Over the Next 10 Years

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Jensen Huang stepped away from the company. I’m still not selling.

Nvidia (NVDA -3.62%) is arguably navigating one of the most challenging periods in the company's history. The tech leader's co-founder and longtime CEO, Jensen Huang, stepped down from his role after it was revealed that he had been unfaithful to his wife with at least two women, including one who is rumored to have been a Chinese spy.

Huang did a wonderful job of leading Nvidia and making it one of the most successful and powerful companies in the world, given its position in the artificial intelligence (AI) value chain. But the stock has declined by 28% over the past month. It could sink even further. Despite all that, I am not planning to sell my shares. In fact, I still think Nvidia could beat the market over the next decade.

There is a reason that Wall Street loves founder-led businesses. Some of the largest and most successful companies in the world became so due in large part to the leadership and vision of their legendary founders. The list includes Apple (AAPL -0.84%), Amazon (AMZN -3.16%), Microsoft (MSFT -0.31%), and Berkshire Hathaway (BRKA +0.11%) (BRKB +0.17%), among others. We can add Nvidia to this list.

However, notice that some of the corporations above performed well long after their founder CEO left. For instance, Bill Gates stepped down as CEO of Microsoft in 2000. The tech company has crushed broader equities ever since. Similarly, Steve Jobs stepped down as Apple's leader in 2011, and the company has performed extremely well ever since. Of course, that's no guarantee that the same will happen to Nvidia.

But while Huang's leadership was instrumental in getting the company to where it is today, Nvidia's business, as it stands, has certain strengths that will survive the departure of its CEO. Nvidia boasts the leading share in the GPU (graphics processing unit) market, technological expertise, a vast portfolio of patents and copyrights, and the CUDA ecosystem -- perhaps its greatest strength -- that grants it a competitive advantage from switching costs.

None of these will simply evaporate. Further, Nvidia arguably has several other high-level executives who know the business inside and out and can do at least a decent job of leading the company through its next chapter.

Ian Buck, the man widely credited with much of the technical work behind the development of CUDA, is one of them. He has been with the company for more than two decades and is currently the vice president and general manager of hyperscale and high-performance computing.

Colette Kress has stepped up as interim CEO. She was previously the company's CFO and has been with the company for over a decade. Perhaps the board will choose someone from outside the company to replace Huang, although the pool of legitimate candidates is probably very small.

Earnings season provided strong evidence that the AI tailwind is far from over. Some hyperscalers continue to spend fortunes on infrastructure, while demand for compute capacity is soaring, as the financial results of neoclouds such as CoreWeave (CRWV -6.49%) showed.

Nvidia remains in an excellent position to capitalize on this over the long run. True, Huang's ability to identify the next potentially massive opportunity will be sorely missed. Even so, he did a wonderful job of transforming Nvidia into a corporation that provides not just GPUs, but a comprehensive, end-to-end AI infrastructure platform. It's also worth emphasizing that Nvidia's financial success in recent years could help it navigate the next chapter.

The company generated $127 billion in free cash flow over the trailing-12-month period. Nvidia has so much cash that it has significantly increased dividends -- by 2,400% -- and share buybacks this year. Nvidia benefits from significant financial flexibility, an asset that investors shouldn't ignore.

It's for all those reasons (and others) that I am not considering selling my shares. I am bullish on the long-term prospects of AI, and I am also convinced that Nvidia's board will do its best to find an excellent replacement for Huang.

Combined with the company's already excellent position in the industry, the stock could still deliver outstanding returns over the next decade, although there is more uncertainty now than there was a week ago. The good news is that the market has adjusted. Nvidia's shares aren't that far from their 52-week lows, and at current levels, the stock looks attractive to hold through 2036.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 823%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 209% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

Milan-Endrick, Bucchioni spiega: "Il problema è Mourinho che non vuole mai mollare i giocatori anche se li utilizza poco"

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nel corso del consueto appuntamento con il suo editoriale per TMW, Enzo Bucchioni non solo ha parlato di Nazionale, ma anche toccato i più caldi temi d'attualità legati alla Serie A, soffermandosi anche sul Milan di Gerry Cardinale. Di seguito il suo pensiero:

" [...] La grande svolta probabilmente arriverà da Cardinale che s’è messo in testa di vincere lo scudetto, quindi ha pronti gli acquisti per sistemare i punti deboli del Milan. Il budget c’è, il nuovo capo dell’area tecnica pure. Come sappiamo, anche se arriverà ufficialmente solo a gennaio, è già operativo il tedesco Markus Krosche, 46 anni, ex Eintracht Francoforte. In Germania ha fatto benissimo, ha sistemi innovativi di valutazione, con una grande rete di scouting. Per gennaio gli obiettivi sono tre, un centravanti dal gol facile, un difensore centrale e un esterno destro di difesa. Può arrivare Tresoldi? Sarebbe un grande colpo per il Milan, ma anche in chiave nazionale. Il ragazzo, 22 anni, ha sempre tifato i rossoneri. Venire in Italia potrebbe anche convincerlo a scegliere la nazionale azzurra come auspicato da Mancini. Il problema, ovviamente, è il Brugge. La squadra belga chiede una cifra attorno ai sessanta milioni e c’è da verificare la possibilità di trattare già per il mese di gennaio. Su tutto questo stanno lavorando Cardinale e Krosche tenendo aperta anche la porta con il Real Madrid per il brasiliano Endrick, 20 anni.In questo caso sarebbe una operazione diversa, un prestito con qualche clausola. Il problema è Mourinho che non vuole mai mollare i giocatori anche se li utilizza pochissimo come Endrick. Il ragazzino preme, l’anno scorso a gennaio era stato prestato e vorrebbe una soluzione analoga. In corsa c’è pure la Roma, ma Cardinale da tempo ha aperto un canale con Florentino Perez".

Napoli, più premi alla squadra nell'ultimo anno di Conte: le cifre dal bilancio 2025/26

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Cresce sensibilmente il monte ingaggi del Napoli. Dal bilancio chiuso al 30 giugno 2026 emerge un incremento significativo delle somme destinate ai calciatori, tra stipendi fissi e bonus legati ai risultati sportivi. I compensi contrattuali sono passati da 100,4 a 128,9 milioni di euro, con un aumento del 28,3%. Considerando anche i premi, la spesa complessiva per i giocatori ha raggiunto quota 138,6 milioni, contro i 106,6 milioni dell'esercizio precedente.

A incidere sul monte ingaggi è stata anche la crescita delle componenti variabili. I premi riconosciuti ai calciatori sono saliti da 6,2 a 9,75 milioni di euro, con un incremento superiore ai 3,5 milioni. Complessivamente, dunque, il club azzurro ha speso circa 32 milioni in più rispetto alla stagione 2024/25 per stipendi e bonus dei propri giocatori.

Diverso l'andamento dei costi relativi agli allenatori, scesi da 22,3 a 19,95 milioni di euro, considerando l'intera categoria e non soltanto il tecnico della prima squadra. In diminuzione anche i compensi di istruttori, tecnici e altri tesserati, passati da 3,98 a 3,33 milioni. Il totale delle retribuzioni dei tesserati ha così raggiunto 161,9 milioni di euro, quasi 29 milioni in più rispetto all'esercizio precedente e pari al 93,9% dei costi complessivi del personale.

When did Scotland last beat Switzerland? Full head to head record and last five games

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Scotland’s Nations League campaign wraps up on Monday evening with a tough encounter against Switzerland as the side aims to snatch an unlikely promotion playoff spot in Group B1 from Slovenia.

Head coach Sebastien Pocognoli finally got his first home win with a 2-1 win over North Macedonia on Friday, and now his team will look to make it back-to-back wins for the first time under his management while hoping Slovenia slip up in the group’s other game.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

However, should Scotland fail to win in St Gallen, hopes of promotion back to League A will be extinguished. A third-place finish will also have ramifications for the upcoming Euro 2028 qualifiers, which are drawn in Belfast this December.

But when did Scotland last beat Switzerland? What is their head-to-head record, and when did they last meet? Here’s everything you need to know about Scotland’s record against Switzerland.

The teams have faced each other a total of 18 times, and have met twice in major tournament finals, with both encounters taking place in the group stage of the European Championships.

They last faced each other just last month in the Nations League, where Switzerland ran out 3-0 winners after Jack Hendry was sent off early on at Hampden Park. Prior to that game, they drew 1-1 at Euro 2024 in Germany, with Scott McTominay opening the scoring early on for Scotland, before Xherdan Shaqiri equalised shortly afterwards.

From their 18 games played, Scotland have eight wins, giving them a 33.3% win percentage. Switzerland have won a total of six times - a 22.22 per cent win percentage. A further four games have ended in a draw.

Despite having the better head to head record against the Swiss, the Scotland men’s national team have not tasted victory against them for over 30 years. Their most recent win came at Euro 1996 at Villa Park, Birmingham on 18 June, where they earned their first win a 1-0 win in their final group game at the tournament.

The victory came via a fine strike from Ally McCoist in the 36th minute, but was not enough for Scotland to qualify for the knockout stages of the tournament, with the Netherlands finishing above them on goal difference. The Switzerland side that day included future Celtic defender Ramon Vega.

Scotland line up that night: Andy Goram, Tom Boyd, Colin Calderwood, Colin Hendry, Stuart McCall, Gary McAllister, John Collins, Tosh McKinlay (Scott Booth, 60), Craig Burley, Gordon Durie and Ally McCoist (John Spencer, 84).

29 September 2026: Scotland 0-3 Switzerland (Nations League)

19 June 2024: Scotland 1-1 Switzerland (Euro 2024)

1 March 2006: Scotland 1-3 Switzerland (Friendly)

18 June 1996: Scotland 1-0 Switzerland (Euro 1996)

8 September 1993: Scotland 1-1 Switzerland (World Cup 1994 Qualifier)

10 Party Members I Benched Immediately (and Still Feel Guilty About)

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Contributor at DualShockers, former Esports Insider DACH content lead, former Escapist Magazine Germany content lead, software engineering background.

I have a complicated relationship with the party menu. In any RPG with more companions than party slots, somebody has to stay behind at camp, and whilst I would love to tell you that I rotate everyone fairly, that would be a lie. Some characters get benched within minutes of joining, and most of them never make it back out.

Whether it's through mid-battle character switching or something a little more esoteric, these JRPGs all make you rethink your party mid-battle!

It is rarely personal. Some of them are too slow, some arrive too late, and some simply have the misfortune of standing next to a character I like more. I do still feel guilty about every single one of them, though, so let’s take a look at ten party members I benched immediately, and why I’m still a little bit sorry about it.

Baldur’s Gate 3 is a very interesting case when it comes to companions: a lot of them start out really, really unlikable. Then you get to know them and their motivations, and it suddenly becomes quite understandable why they act the way that they do. Larian Studios did that so well that benching anyone feels a little bit like abandoning a friend.

Gale is the one I leave at camp regardless. I don’t mind his storyline at all, but I simply don’t enjoy the way wizards work, with every spell slot spent bringing you one step closer to yet another long rest. I usually play a Warlock myself, and having a reliable cantrip like Eldritch Blast means that I never have to worry about that kind of balancing act, especially early on.

It also doesn’t help that there are plenty of companions I like a lot more. Gale’s personality is just a bit goofy for my taste, and whilst I did keep him around for one full playthrough to properly get to know him and his story, I have never felt the need to repeat that. He is a perfectly lovely man. He’s just a perfectly lovely man who waits at camp.

Blue mages have always been one of the more interesting concepts in Final Fantasy. Their magic tends to be really powerful, since there has to be a reason to use them over your regular black, white and even red mages, and that usually means they go from being a bit weak to being suddenly wildly overpowered. Kimahri should be exactly that kind of character.

The problem is that his blue magic is tied to his Overdrive. He learns enemy abilities with Lancet, but can only use them as his Ronso Rage, and whilst there are ways to charge Overdrives faster later on, it makes him awkward for a large part of the game. Everyone else in the party already has a clearly defined role, too, and Kimahri sits in his own odd little corner of the Sphere Grid without one.

Now, I tend to 100% every Final Fantasy, so he does get used eventually. If I’m simply building a party, though, he is the last character on my mind, which is a shame. Truth be told, the story doesn’t help, either: as Yuna’s guardian, he barely speaks early on, and that makes it hard to form an emotional connection with him. His character design is absolutely sick, mind you.

Sharla is a trap, and I say that with love. When she first joins in Xenoblade Chronicles, she is a pretty good character: a dedicated healer with some decent ether arts, at a point where you have nobody else to bench her for. That is precisely where the problem starts.

Because she feels essential early on, you keep her, even as better options join. She deals next to no damage, though, which means that fights take longer, and longer fights mean that you take more damage. All of that damage then gets healed by Sharla, who ends up looking far more useful than she actually is.

I fell for it on my first playthrough, and I have seen plenty of others do exactly the same. A dedicated healer just isn’t all that necessary once you double down on damage or find more flexible healing elsewhere, like Shulk’s Light Heal. Sharla has been on my bench ever since, and I do feel bad about it, since none of this is really her fault.

From controversial clowns to big blue lionmen, Final Fantasy has party members you remember for being utterly useless but who are anything but!

For the longest time, I was shackled by what party composition makes sense for the story, and I still struggle with letting go of that sometimes. You will never catch me playing a Pokémon game without my starter, for example. In a similar vein, many of my Tales of Symphonia playthroughs simply ended up with Lloyd, Colette, Raine and Genis, because they are the ones who have known each other from the very beginning.

Eventually, I managed to get over myself. Since then, without fail, Genis has never been in my party again. I like the conversations between him and Lloyd, and I do believe that pairing a carefree character with a snarkier, more considerate one works out really well.

I just usually find him a bit grating. That is a harsh thing to say about a child who has been through as much as Genis has, and I’m aware of that every time I move him out of the party. It hasn’t stopped me yet.

Kongol always ends up benched. It’s common knowledge that Rose falls off quite aggressively on Disc 2, and again on Disc 4, which is unfortunate, but even she has seen more action in my playthroughs than he has. For the last surviving Giganto, that has to sting.

My issue is simple: I don’t usually enjoy very slow characters, and Kongol is incredibly strong but painfully slow. To make him properly useful, you have to boost both his speed and his magic defence quite aggressively, and I would rather spend that effort on someone who doesn’t need it. He never stood much of a chance with me.

Luckily, The Legend of Dragoon isn’t a particularly hard game at the best of times, so you can beat it with any party you like. That takes some of the guilt away. Kongol saved Dart and his friends from a falling pillar before joining them, though, and I repaid him with the better part of four discs spent sitting around.

It pains me so much to say this, because I love dogs! In Dragon Age: Origins, however, Dog is close to useless compared to anyone else. Unless you install a mod, he takes up a full party slot, just like any other companion would, and he can’t carry a fight the way they can.

He does a semi-decent job in the first few levels, since Alistair and Sten aren’t great tanks at that stage, either. He then gets outscaled quickly, as your warriors pick up their defensive talents and proper tank gear, and on Nightmare difficulty, that gap becomes very hard to ignore. Dog isn’t beefy enough to be a tank and doesn’t deal enough damage to make up for it, which leaves him without a clear role.

His Overwhelm ability is devastating against a single dangerous target, mages in particular, but that is about it. It’s a real shame, because his interactions with the other companions are often hilarious. Leaving him at camp means missing out on those, and it never gets any easier.

What is interesting, though, is that there exists a Extra Dog Slot mod that lets him tag along as a fifth party member, and it shows up on nearly every list of essential mods for the game. I am clearly not the only one with a guilty conscience.

Goofy has done nothing wrong, and I want that on record. My problem with the first Kingdom Hearts is that nearly every world offers a guest character who can take Donald’s or Goofy’s place, and I love how unique that makes each world feel. Somebody has to make room, and it was usually Goofy.

Recently, I felt really bad about that, and I wanted to make it right in my latest playthrough. Sadly, all it did was make me realise that the guest characters are a big part of the game’s charm for me. Fighting alongside Ariel or the Beast is simply too cool to pass up, and since they can’t leave their home worlds, it always feels like now or never.

So Goofy might have to sit this one out a little longer. He will take it well, because he is Goofy. That only makes it worse.

With a single action or word, there are moments in these Nintendo DS JRPGs Where Companions Can Derail the Entire Story.

Jean is the most painful example of wasted potential I can think of. His design is memorable, his personality is fun, and being the frog prince of SimaFort sets him apart from everyone else in Breath of Fire II. He believes that there are no bad people in the world, only bad hearts, and he says all of it in a faux-French accent.

Mechanically, sadly, he has very little to offer. He joins slow and underwhelming, with good defence but incredibly low agility, and his stats never match the way he looks. His Holy fusion turns him into a tall, heroic-looking frog knight who can in theory become quite formidable, but it comes too late and with too many trade-offs to justify his slot over characters who have already proven themselves.

Jean isn’t unplayable. He is a “what could have been”: a character who looks like he should be iconic in battle, but never gets the chance to live up to it. If he had been a little more like Frog in Chrono Trigger, with some healing to offer, he might have been worthwhile well before the endgame.

Yangus is easily one of the most lovable characters in Dragon Quest VIII, and I still barely use him for long stretches of the game. He is the dedicated tank, and the job gradually overwhelms him: magic can lower his armour, most of his equipment reduces his evasion, and he is very slow. He has a respectable Strength base, but not much gets built on top of it.

In the 3DS version, which added Red and Morrie as party members, that makes him the obvious candidate for the sidelines once the new recruits join. He can become useful again in the late game, but that means building his Scythe tree from the ground up. That is a lot to ask for a character I have already replaced.

It isn’t all doom and gloom, since he simply dips in the mid-game. Early on, 16 skill points in Humanity get you Nose for Treasure, which tells you how many chests are left in an area, and that is quite useful. I just wish that was enough to keep him in the party.

Nino joins with such a good story that you really want to use her and help her grow. Raised by the Black Fang, she is sent to assassinate Prince Zephiel in Battle Before Dawn, has a change of heart and ends up fleeing with Jaffar instead. By the time she joins you, it’s impossible not to root for her.

Unfortunately, she arrives as a Level 5 Mage very late in the game, when you are most likely fielding promoted units. Her growth rates are amongst the highest in the game, so she will catch up if you put the time in, but she’s a glass cannon until then, and there isn’t much game left. Unless you wrap your whole strategy around her, she gets benched immediately. This makes her a classic example of what Fire Emblem fans call the Est archetype: a unit that joins late and underlevelled, but with enormous potential.

Sometimes, it feels like her sole purpose is to stand between you and recruiting Jaffar, since he only joins if she talks to him and they both survive. This tiny mage is brimming with potential, and I have made use of it in some playthroughs. I’m just not always in the mood for that, so more often than not, she ends up on the bench.

Crisp air, hot coffee and a cozy sofa: autumn is when I reach for nostalgia, and these ten GameCube JRPGs still make the trip back worthwhile.

“Train Daddy” Morgan Spector Is Suddenly a Sex Symbol

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

At 46, ‘The Gilded Age’ star is becoming a leading man as the new Robert Langdon — and an object of internet obsession.

Twenty years after Morgan Spector arrived in New York hoping to make a living as an actor, he has finally reached the point where he’s No. 1 on the call sheet. All it took was becoming a sex symbol at 46.

After years spent bouncing between theater and prestige television, Spector’s turn as railroad baron George Russell on HBO’s The Gilded Age has made him an object of internet lust — “Train Daddy,” to a particularly enthusiastic segment of the show’s fan base — and given him the sort of platform he’s still figuring out what to do with. And now comes an even bigger leap: Spector is taking over as Robert Langdon, the globe-trotting Harvard symbologist Tom Hanks played in three blockbuster movies, in Netflix’s upcoming adaptation of Dan Brown’s The Secret of Secrets. It’s easily the biggest role of Spector’s career. Related Stories TV 'The Gilded Age' Is Serving Up Ambition and Shame in Season 4 Return Movies 'Moonfish' Review: Morgan Spector and Lucy Liu Voice a Spare but Evocative Animated Sci-Fi Suffused With Loneliness

Although Spector is quick to qualify the achievement. “But my wife is No. 2,” he says, referring to spouse and Secrets co-star Rebecca Hall. “So I don’t get to eat all the green M&Ms on set, if you know what I mean.”

Spector is currently experiencing one of the more interesting midcareer moments in Hollywood. Until relatively recently, he seemed comfortably settled into being one of those reliably excellent performers whose face audiences might recognize even if his name didn’t immediately come to mind.

His growing celebrity has presented another complication. “My era of becoming somebody with a platform has also been a time of dramatic political change, and I’m an outspoken person,” Spector says. “Sometimes I’d like to get off social media, but then I’m abandoning a space that I can use to say things that matter. But then, I have to do all the things that make people want to come to the platform, which means taking pretty pictures of yourself. There’s only so much reposting of ‘Democracy Now’ that your average Instagram viewer wants  to see.”

In the years before The Gilded Age, Spector was doing what actors have traditionally done when nobody was asking them for selfies: looking for work. After graduating from Reed College and the American Conservatory Theater in San Francisco, he moved to New York, where in 2010 a two-line walk-on in the Broadway production of A View From the Bridge turned into a starring role opposite Liev Schreiber and Scarlett Johansson.

“I was living off Nostrand Avenue in Brooklyn at the time, and I would commute in to work every day in a state of euphoria,” he says. “It just felt like New York City was wrapping its arms around me and giving me all I’ve ever dreamed of. But then I had to calibrate and think, ‘OK, well, what else is possible now?’ “

For a while, the answer was more work rather than stardom. Roles on Homeland and miniseries The Plot Against America followed, and when Spector auditioned to play George Russell on The Gilded Age, he saw the Julian Fellowes drama as a chance to add another solid credit — and, with any luck, a steady job — to his résumé.

“They originally talked about five seasons as being a way to complete the arc,” Spector says. “But I saw that [HBO head of drama] Francesca Orsi say she’d love it to run for 10 years, so who knows?”

What he didn’t foresee was the way audiences would respond to George. “Somebody wrote a piece about how George and Bertha’s [Carrie Coon] marriage was the sexiest thing on television, and I remember thinking, ‘What?!’ ” he says with a laugh. “The show is relatively chaste, especially for HBO, and we weren’t really lingering in eroticism. But it was the first sign that, OK, people are connecting to this in a way that wasn’t obvious to us as we were making it.”

Then came Robert Langdon. When Spector read for The Secret of Secrets with showrunner Carlton Cuse, he was reminded of his Gilded Age audition — another opportunity he wasn’t entirely sure made sense for him.

“I didn’t think I was right for the part, and I guess as I’m getting older, I’m realizing that I don’t know what’s going to work for me or what I’m going to be good at,” Spector adds with a laugh.

He is now filming The Secret of Secrets in Prague. The acting part, at least, he knows how to handle. The rest of his newfound celebrity is proving more complicated.

“They don’t teach you about this part in school,” he says. “It’s like, how many thirst traps are enough and how many are too many? We’re making our way through the wilderness.”

You Can Try WhatsApp's New Disappearing Text Messages Early

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Like Snapchat messages, WhatsApp's "View Once" feature is a great way to send things you don't want living on your friends' devices. You can send voice messages, photos, and videos that will "self-destruct" after they're opened, limiting (if not eliminating) the risk of your friends keeping them forever. However, View Once has a major limitation: While WhatsApp will let you send voice memos and images that way, you can't use it to send texts, meaning every one you send will live on (unless you unsend it yourself).

That's now changing. As reported by WABetaInfo, WhatsApp is now beta testing View Once for text messages, too. Soon enough, you'll be able to send voice memos, photos, videos, and texts as one-time-only messages. That way, you can send sensitive information via text—say, a password or an important account number—without worrying about it persisting. Here's how it works: You craft your text message as you usually do, but rather than tap the send button, you hold it. You'll see the usual "Schedule message" option, but once the feature rolls out, you'll also find a new "View Once" option as well.

As WABetaInfo highlights, the first time you use this feature, WhatsApp will present a pop-up explaining the feature, how "View Once text gives you more privacy," and that when you send the message, it'll disappear from the chat after the recipient opens it. WhatsApp also explains that recipients cannot share, forward, save, or take screenshots of View Once text messages. Recipients won't see the text appear in the usual chat interface, either; instead, your recipient will need to open the message in a separate window. Once they close this page, the message disappears.

As mentioned above, this feature is currently in testing, so it's only available to WhatsApp beta testers. What's more, only the Android version of the beta has access at the moment, so iOS beta testers are out of luck. (That said, the WhatsApp iOS beta is perpetually full, so there's not much you could do even if WhatsApp made this test available on iPhone.)

If you have an Android device, you can enroll in the WhatsApp beta to try out "View Once" text messages today. According to WABetaInfo, the feature is live as of WhatsApp version 2.26.45.6. To enroll, open the WhatsApp beta page on the Google Play store and enroll your account in the beta. If you don't have WhatsApp on your phone already, download it here; if you do, reload the page and install the latest update. This will update your existing WhatsApp app to the latest beta version.

Instagram creators can now tag 30 Amazon products in a single Reel

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

US creators can tag up to 30 Amazon products in a Reel and earn a commission on each sale.

Instagram now lets US creators tag Amazon products in Reels and posts.

Instagram now lets US creators in Amazon’s influencer program add Amazon affiliate links directly to Reels and feed posts, with up to 30 products per Reel. Meta takes no cut. The feature was first promised in March and arrives just before Black Friday.

Instagram creators in the US can now add Amazon affiliate links straight to their Reels and feed posts, Meta announced on Monday. Viewers who tap a product go to the Amazon app, and the creator earns a commission on any sale, TechCrunch reported.

Creators can tag up to 30 Amazon products in a Reel and 20 in a feed post. They link their Amazon account in Instagram’s settings, and the app shows the expected commission for each item before they post. Meta says it takes no cut and charges no fees.

For now, the feature is only for US creators aged 18 or older who have a professional account and are in Amazon’s influencer program, according to Engadget. Meta has not said if it will reach other countries.

Meta says links on posts get about twice as many clicks as links in a creator’s bio. That is bad news for the “link in bio” services many influencers use, because Instagram has long limited where links can go. Paid Meta One plans now also let creators put links in their posts.

Meta may not take a cut, but the links tell it what its users want to buy, Engadget noted.

The feature has been a while coming. Meta first showed 30-product tagging for Reels in March and said Amazon links would reach Instagram “this spring”. Facebook creators in the US have been able to earn from Amazon, eBay and Temu links since then.

Instagram is fighting TikTok and YouTube for creators. Tools built for TikTok Shop sellers are moving to Instagram too. Instagram is also reportedly offering YouTubers up to $500K to post long videos to the app.

For Amazon, the links pull more shoppers into its own app, where it wants its Alexa for Shopping agent to become the main way people buy.

Gravemark is a roguelike dungeon crawler you can play in portrait mode, out now on iOS

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ever since 198X's Rogue, the idea of venturing into a dungeon, grabbing loot and attempting to exit without being killed has thrilled hardcore players. No surprise then, that Gravemark, a portrait-mode roguelike, has really caught our eye. And now it's not just Android players able to enjoy it, as Gravemark hits iOS!

At its core, Gravemark is your stereotypical roguelike. Venture into the deep dungeon, automatically battle enemies while selecting skills and potions, then decide what to put into your limited inventory space before choosing whether to plunge deeper or get out. The twists are the aforementioned auto-battles, limited inventory, and the fact you don't actually know what the loot is until you take it back to be identified.

About the only unfortunate thing about Gravemark is that, you guessed it, there are hints of AI about it, especially with some of the key art. It's a shame because the core gameplay seems rock solid. And while I'm sure for some it won't detract, for me it does colour my perception of the game a bit.

If you're willing to give Gravemark a go regardless, you can find it out now on iOS and available already via Android, free to play.

Or, if you'd prefer a slightly more traditional (and forgiving) experience, why not check out our list of the best RPGs on Android, for adventures through worlds of fantasy and sci-fi?

I Took a Mystery “Decision Detox” Trip With Zero Planning. Here’s What Happened.

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

From the treehouse-like deck outside my hotel room, I took in the lush cluster of live oak and eucalyptus trees all around me, the whisper of wind and chirp of unknown birds, and the small sliver of Pacific Ocean peeking through the foliage-lined horizon. I was completely at peace. The best part: I hadn’t made a single decision for the entire weekend. In fact, until just a couple days prior—the day of my trip departure—I had absolutely no idea where I’d be on this gorgeous Sunday morning.

The morning of my trip, I tore open a sealed envelope that held all the information about the hotel where I’d be staying (Chaminade Resort and Spa), my itinerary (forest therapy, spa treatments, dining at The View and Lago di Como), and even the destination itself (Santa Cruz, California). This was all part of the “mystery travel” experience I eagerly agreed to, facilitated by Benchmark Resorts & Hotels and Pack Up + Go, an agency that plans completely curated surprise vacations. Here was the deal: I filled out a survey about my preferences (my priorities while traveling, the types of hotels I liked) along with budget, then they planned the entire trip, and I wouldn’t find out details (beyond some notes about weather and packing suggestions) until I opened my envelope the morning of my departure.

As an avid traveler, eldest daughter, and strategic planner (particularly in the context of trips), I thought this format might stress me out—but I found there was a sense of freedom and lightness in not pouring into pre-trip research or curating my own itinerary. All I had to do was show up. This brand of mental surrender actually speaks to a rising travel trend: “decision detox.”

Modern life can often feel like an endless series of choices. Between social media, the internet, and now AI, “we are inundated with information and access to it,” says Morgan Cutlip, PhD, psychology educator, author, and researcher. “This sends us regularly searching for the ‘right’ way of doing things rather than trusting our instincts or talking to a friend and doing what they do.”

And that absolutely applies to trip planning. “When there are so many options for something like a vacation, the more information you consume and the more reviews you read, the more uncertain you can become about your decision,” she says. “Searching out accommodations, looking up flight options, thinking through food—it becomes a ton of micro decisions packed into this one major thing that feels like it’s supposed to be extra magical, extra fun, and low stress, so the stakes feel high.”

In fact, while vacations are meant to be a time to unwind and enjoy life, a 2025 survey of 1,500 Americans from YouGov found 70 percent of U.S. vacationers find their booking experience to be stress-inducing. That’s where “decision detox” trips come in, where you completely relinquish control of trip planning. The intention here is for travelers to take a break from the mental overload of everyday life and never-ending choices, in order to take a true vacation.

This travel trend is on the rise. A research report published by Lemongrass, a certified B-Corp marketing agency, found a clear shift in traveler preferences for curated, all-inclusive itineraries that remove planning fatigue; a desire to visit quieter, less-crowded destinations; and a pull toward peaceful relaxation over highly stimulating experiences.

And while traditional travel agents have long been planning accommodations and transportation, decision detox trips take that concept one step further with an emphasis on fully planned retreats and completely handled logistics. In my case, this meant a complete surprise trip and fully embracing the thrill of adventure that comes with not knowing your destination until the moment you depart.

“For some people, this might sound like handcuffs on a holiday, but for [people] burdened with making seemingly every decision in their families’ lives all the time, it’s heaven in the form of a pre-booked massage and meals that magically appear when desired,” Jools Sampson, founder and owner of Reclaim Yourself Retreats, wrote in the Lemongrass report.

Women are likely disproportionately affected by this kind of decision fatigue, as they tend to take on the lion’s share of trip planning. In fact, data from research organization Skift found that 82 percent of all travel decisions are made by women. This can be particularly draining for moms, who are constantly managing the logistics of life, says Cutlip, whose work often focuses on mental load discrepancies in relationships. “And if she’s the one planning it, she’s almost certainly coordinating with her partner too. Not being able to fully make decisions on her own, having to negotiate and talk each one through, adds yet another layer of work.”

But no matter what role someone takes in a family structure, or what the underlying reason is for mental fatigue, leaning into decision detox travel can come with a whole slew of mental health benefits.

“You eliminate all of the fatigue, overwhelm, and unnecessary stress that can come with planning a trip,” says Cutlip. “If you’re somebody who has the capacity to let go and you’re comfortable putting someone else in the driver’s seat, it frees you to focus on everything else a vacation requires: preparing to leave your home, getting work off, taking care of the pets, cleaning out the fridge, and then showing up ready to relax.”

Benchmark decided to partner with Pack Up + Go after reading YouGov’s survey about traveler planning fatigue. “It’s a different way to approach a vacation,” says Evan Crawford, vice president of marketing for Benchmark Resorts & Hotels.

It also leads to travelers visiting locations they may not have otherwise thought to book—such as Woodinville, Washington, or the Columbia River Gorge, says Crawford. “Maybe you’ve never even heard of the place before, but now you can go there and get exposed to it and have this extraordinary experience.”

And while a complete surprise vacation is one approach, it’s not the only way to try out a decision detox trip.

A reprieve from decision-making is at the heart of Duvine Cycling, which curates bike tour vacations that take guests everywhere from the vineyards of Burgundy to the Southern Lakes region of New Zealand.

“Basically, all you have to do is show up on a trip. You don’t have to think about anything,” says CEO and co-founder Andy Levine. These active, planned trips inherently encourage people to unplug and make lasting connections with their fellow cyclists. “[We act as] their concierge, and we get to make these really cool things happen, all while on a bike.”

Then there’s Selar, a sustainable polar expedition company launching in early 2027. For anyone seeking a grand adventure—without all the intricate planning that often goes into these types of trips—Selar offers a chance to take a journey way up north with itineraries across Norway, Svalbard, and Greenland. Or take HX Expeditions, which, similar to a cruise model, offers all-inclusive voyages that cover excursions, meals, drinks, and gratuities—but unlike a classic cruise, these itineraries are led by onboard naturalists and historians, and their packages also include lectures, hands-on experiences, and expedition gear.

At Vermejo, a Ted Turner Reserve in New Mexico, each guest’s nature-focused itinerary is completely handled by their Reserve Ambassadors. There are more than 45 guided activities available, ranging from horseback riding through vast prairies, fly-fishing for Rio Grande cutthroat trout, or taking a UTV tour with the opportunity to spot elk, bear, and other big game.

Of course, numerous wellness resorts have been hopping on this trend for some time now, acknowledging that a true mental reset is intrinsically linked to less decision-making. BodyHoliday, an all-inclusive wellness resort in St. Lucia, asks guests to “give us your body for a week, and we’ll give you back your mind;” Sensei Lanai in Hawaii offers guided stays that cater to your individual health needs and goals; and at Renew Retreat at Calamigos Ranch in California, you can take part in a preset wellness retreat.

There are also a number of retreats specifically designed for staying active. Take a running retreat with Run Wild, for example: these women-only trips are designed so you simply choose your pace and place of interest and select an already-set itinerary.

The crux of these trips is rooted in analog experiences, which can help with the mental fatigue and technology overload we experience in everyday life. “I think people are really craving that opportunity to be in the moment, to be human,” says Crawford.

Surrendering complete control is never easy—but in the case of my trip to Santa Cruz, I found it wildly therapeutic. By not stressing about every detail, both before and during the trip, I was able to feel fully present with every item on my itinerary, including a hike through redwood forests, idly reading by the pool in my (pre-reserved) cabana, and savoring meals set to stunning views of the treetops and ocean beyond.

Because I opted for a road trip rather than air travel, the Pack Up + Go team even chose my rest stop on the way from Los Angeles (Paso Robles), along with recommendations for restaurants and shops to explore in the area. And this detailed research extended to my final destination in Santa Cruz, too: the team provided a meticulously curated list of shops, experiences, and more for me to explore during my final day in the area.

Before embarking on your own decision detox trip, take stock of whether this will feel freeing or cause you more stress. “My advice would be to reflect on whether you’re somebody who can actually let go of control,” says Cutlip. “If you’re going to turn it over to someone else and then micromanage the process, just do it yourself. If you can turn over control, fully embrace it and enjoy it.”

For me, it was a practice in letting go of control and allowing outside help—something I think many of us could benefit from on occasion. And while I won’t necessarily be handing over the reins for every future trip I take, it was a beautiful opportunity to give my mind a bit of reprieve, to stop overthinking for a while, and simply take in the wonder of a beautiful place.

Microsoft fixes Windows activation as admins pick through Exchange outage wreckage

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Another unscheduled patch for IT teams already having quite enough fun with Redmond

Microsoft has released an out-of-band update to fix licensing and activation errors introduced by November's Windows security update.

The fix is available from the Microsoft Update Catalog for Windows Server 2025, 2022, and 2019, Windows 10 22H2 ESU, and Windows 10 Enterprise LTSC 2021 and 2019. Windows 11 23H2 users can obtain it from the Catalog or through Windows Update's Optional updates menu.

The "non-security OOB update" targets affected enterprise devices. Microsoft stated: "Certain Windows licensing and activation operations using the Software Licensing Management Tool (SLMGR) might fail with error 0x80041008 (Invalid parameter).

"This primarily affects organizations using slmgr.vbs commands or automated processes to configure Key Management Service (KMS) activation, install product keys, or manage Windows licensing."

Devices that are already activated will remain activated, Microsoft said.

Breaking the machinery customers use to activate paid-for software is a bad look for a Patch Tuesday release. Administrators may wonder how Microsoft's updates are tested when installing one means waiting for another to repair the damage.

Those same administrators may also have been fielding calls about last week's widespread Exchange Online outage, which Microsoft has partly attributed to a defect it introduced in July.

On top of that, Microsoft told administrators just days ago to consider unsupported Exchange Server 2016 and 2019 "permanently vulnerable." Exchange Online avoids that particular support deadline, but the recent outage was a reminder that handing over the servers doesn't make software defects disappear.

Forget "security by obscurity." Last week's approach was more like "security by unavailability."

Between getting Windows activated and keeping Exchange Online online, Microsoft gave administrators plenty to do. None of it was likely to feature in their original plans for the week.

Not all the glitches introduced by November's Patch Tuesday have yet been resolved, as some customers using a Windows device to print will be aware. ®

Blizzard changes WoW: Forever dungeon XP again, admits level restrictions "missed the mark"

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

"There should be much less nullification happening now"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

World of Warcraft: Forever has suffered from a dungeon experience-shaped problem since its Beta period, and Blizzard is well aware of fans' thoughts regarding it – so much so, in fact, that its latest hotfixes attempt to remedy some of the negative feedback.

In case you're out of the loop, dungeon XP in World of Warcraft: Forever has been a hot topic for quite some time now thanks to Blizzard's nerfs. The studio has been listening, though, and recently introduced a 50% boost to the experience points folks gain from kills in dungeons – but the changes didn't end there. Developers went on to nullify all XP gained from an enemy in a dungeon should a player's level be too high. It wasn't an entirely welcome change, to put things lightly, however, leading to yet another quick tweak from Blizzard. Associate production director Clay Stone explains as much over on Twitter, writing, "We just made a change to ease the recent restrictions on XP earned from enemies in dungeons in WoW: Forever. There should be much less nullification happening now." He links to the official blog post on said change.

We just made a change to ease the recent restrictions on XP earned from enemies in dungeons in WoW Forever. There should be much less nullification happening now. Thanks for the feedback, all! Full blue post here:November 15, 2026

"Yesterday, we updated dungeon play so that experience granted by an enemy in an instance is nullified if a high-level player is in the group and the enemy is trivial to that player," it reads. "For some dungeons, the new restriction missed the mark because of the overall level range of the enemies in the dungeon." That's why, as Blizzard goes on to state, new hotfixes have been implemented to loosen this restriction a bit.

"With hotfixes that are now live, we've just deployed a further change to this restriction, making it so that the XP nullification only kicks in when the high-level player is more than 12 levels above the level of the enemies," the devs conclude – and, unsurprisingly, fans are pretty happy to hear it. "I think this was the only thing needed to make this change fantastic," one comment under the forum post asserts. " +12 levels is enough to curtail boosting, but not enough to impact players normally trying to compete quests in the megadungeons." Another adds, "Thank you. That’s more reasonable." The consensus seems to largely be the same on other discussion boards online, too, like those on Reddit, where somebody jokes, "Alright guys lets find something else to complain about now." In other words, GG, Blizzard – this one's going over well.

As for what the future of dungeons and the grind for XP in World of Warcraft: Forever will look like… well, that remains to be seen. I'm pretty content with questing away to get my Druid a bit higher as it stands now, but I'm excited to see what Blizzard's still got in store.

Blizzard knows World of Warcraft: Forever has a gold problem – but that doesn't mean a feature everyone used to grind it in the OG MMO is coming back

Rhode’s Annual Birthday Collection Is Here, and It Features the Brand’s First-Ever Apparel

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Being the giving Sagittarius she is, Hailey Bieber actually gives us something every year for her birthday: a new Rhode collection. Sometimes it’s a special-edition shade or flavor of her award-winning Peptide Lip Tint. Other years, it’s a brand-new product, like the Peptide Eye Prep Hydrogel Patches and a new snap-on lip case. But the 1996-er went all out for her 30th birthday, dropping new products, exclusive styles and colors on some of the brand’s go-to items, and even its first-ever apparel.

Today, Rhode announced Pocket Plush, the brand’s new matte blush in two shades: Baldwin, a berry rose that’s allegedly Hailey’s go-to shade, and Tucson, a rich burgundy. For now, it comes in a limited-edition mini navy packaging that’s v cute (and has been teased for a while on Rhode’s IG). What makes it different from Pocket Blush? Well, the creamy stick is said to leave a velvety, soft matte finish on skin. Think blurring and smoothing, not dewy and fresh like the OG. You can also wear them on cheeks and lips, which Hailey loves to pair together for a monochromatic look.

But that’s not even the most exciting new launch, IMO. For the first time ever, Rhode is launching apparel. It’s only on pre-order right now, but the HB Windbreaker was inspired by Hailey’s love of vintage sportswear. It’s an easy “throw on and go,” oversized jacket that can be worn two ways. There’s navy nylon on one side and gray fleece on the other. If you wanted to match your Rhode phone case, lip balm, and Glazing Milk, now’s your chance. I’m pretty confident this one will sell out, especially ahead of gifting season.

Oh! And that’s not all! (Told ya, Hailey went all out this year.) There’s a limited-edition flavor of the Peptide Lip Boost, a rich, moisturizing lip mask that Cosmo editors have loved since it launched. This one has a shimmery sheer pink tint and a cherry vanilla cola scent. Sounds delicious.

Plus, there are exclusive styles of the Peptide Eye Prep, both in navy to match the collection. You can also grab the Rhode Carry Case and Snap-On Lip Case, both now available in navy just for Hailey’s bday.

Of course, if you wanted to pick up a set to gift (ahem, as a fellow Sag, I can confirm anyone wIth a bday coming up soon would love it), grab The Birthday Set: it contains both shades of Pocket Plush and the Peptide Lip Boost in Cherry Vanilla Cola.

Everything launches (including the windbreaker preorder) on Friday, November 20, at 9 a.m. PT. As all Rhode things go, these will absolutely sell out quickly. If you have your eye on something, I wouldn’t wait.

Beth Gillette is the beauty editor at Cosmopolitan, where she covers skincare, makeup, hair, nails, and more across digital and print. She can generally be found in bright eyeshadow furiously typing her latest feature or hemming and hawing about a new product you "have to try." Prior to Cosmopolitan, she wrote and edited beauty content as an Editor at The Everygirl for four years. Follow her on Instagram for makeup selfies and a new hair 'do every few months.

Half-Life 2 writer's biggest regret after 12 years at Valve is not making fun of Gabe Newell's shirt

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the GamesRadar community for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Former Valve developer Chet Faliszek – writer behind the likes of Half-Life 2, Portal and its sequel, Team Fortress 2, Left 4 Dead, and even Counter-Strike: Global Offensive – has one big regret about his time at the Steam company: a joke he didn't make.

Yes, you read that right. He reveals as much in a new short over on YouTube, asking, "What's the thing I regret the most at Valve? I was there for 12 years. So, what's the thing that I regret, that I did or didn't do?" Apparently, after sitting on this question for some time, he's become brave enough to publicly share his answer – an answer he thinks of "almost every year," as it weighs that heavy in his mind. Brace yourselves for this recollection, y'all.

"If you remember, Valve had Steam Dev Days. We actually had a really early Steam Dev Days where I spoke at, and I spoke horribly, and I made a note that I've got to learn how to speak in public. I'm still not good at it, but it was just so bad. I was just shaking. It was just horrible. So, at this point, when Steam Dev Days two comes around, I'm like, 'Hey, I'll help out.'" Valve then decided to make him the host.

The joke Faliszek mustered up was "going to put Gabe on the spot" – a joke about his polo shirt, to be precise. "My joke was, 'And here's Gabe Newell modeling the latest in polo,' because he always wore a polo shirt. 'Today's selection is polo after dark.'" The dev knew Newell would "lean into it" had he said it, but he kept it to himself and laughed "so hard" without ever, uh, actually making the joke out loud.

Apparently that's nothing new for him, as he's often "laughing at jokes I don't say out loud because I'm thinking, 'That's going to push it.'" He does, again, regret not making this one in particular, though. "I regret that. I should have said that joke. That was a good joke. I know it's not the greatest joke, but at the time with Gabe walking in his black polo, it would have worked." And, honestly, I'd have to agree.

To this day, I seldom ever see Newell not wearing a polo shirt… if ever. I hope he tunes into Faliszek's channel and hears the joke, however, even if it's a good few years after it should've been spoken out loud.

'We've Heard You!' - Star Wars Galactic Racer Is Officially Adding Split-Screen Multiplayer

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

An incredible addition to an incredible game

Star Wars Galactic Racer is my favourite game of the year so far. It's an incredible racing title that even surpasses the original Star Wars Episode 1 Racer in my view, and I've had so much fun with the campaign and online multiplayer.

There's just one downside that everyone's been pointing out since it was revealed, and that's the absence of local split-screen multiplayer. With that in mind, the development team have found a way to implement it in a new update.

The update isn't available yet, but will be coming to the game in early 2027. Alongside it will be a new mode called Free Play, which is where you'll be able to freely choose your vehicle, character, planet and race track.

"We’ve heard you! Local split-screen multiplayer racing is coming in early 2027. A dedicated team at Fuse Games has been working hard to bring a brand new mode, Free Play, to STAR WARS: Galactic Racer. This new mode will allow you to choose your vehicle, character, planet, and race track to race alone or with up to 3 other players in local split-screen action."

It seems Free Play is the only place where you'll be able to access local split-screen multiplayer in Star Wars Galactic Racer, but that's no surprise considering how solo-focused the main campaign is. They'd literally have to rewrite massive portions of the campaign if they were going to implement split-screen into it.

Anyway, this is just an amazing bonus on top of an already amazing game, and I encourage you to check it out if you enjoy a title like Burnout 3: Takedown - it's fast-paced and frantic, has the same kind of takedown system that you'll remember from that game, and the roguelite campaign is really well designed (online is great fun as well).

If you've played it, let us know what you think of the game in the poll down below!

ISC2 AI Security Academy Builds Cybersecurity Professionals' AI Security Skills

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Three learning tracks enable cybersecurity professionals to focus on their AI security responsibilities and professional goals

ALEXANDRIA, Va., Nov. 16, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- ISC2 â the world's leading nonprofit member organization for cybersecurity professionals â today launched the ISC2 AI Security Academy, a structured professional development framework designed to help cybersecurity professionals build practical capabilities for securing AI-enabled environments. The Academy features clear learning pathways â including certificates, courses and workshops â that are mapped to three learning tracks, each focused on a different set of AI security responsibilities.

As AI changes governance, risk management, security operations, software security and more, cybersecurity professionals need ongoing, role-relevant AI security learning to keep up with the pace of change. According to the latest ISC2 Cybersecurity Workforce Study, 41% of participants who said their organization's security team had at least one skill need cited AI skills as the most pressing need.

"Understanding AI's opportunities and risks is only a starting point. Professionals need relevant AI security learning they can put to work in their own roles," said Casey Marks, Ph.D., ISC2 Chief Operations Officer. "The Academy gives them a place to start that reflects their responsibilities and experience. Rather than following the same path, cybersecurity professionals can tailor their AI security development to build on what they have already learned and continue developing skills as roles evolve."

A Commitment to Skilling the Cybersecurity Workforce for AI

The Academy is an important step in ISC2's commitment to give its members and the broader cybersecurity community the resources and skills they need to succeed in the age of AI. Over the past year, ISC2 has expanded the courses, workshops, webinars and research available to its members.

Earlier this year, ISC2 also released Exam Guidance for Artificial Intelligence to show how AI-related skills connect to its certifications. ISC2 is also in the final phase of developing an AI security certification, shaped by nearly 12,000 volunteers worldwide. The pilot exam outline is now available, and 3,000 volunteers will be invited to take the pilot exam later this year. Alongside these efforts, ISC2 has established the Cyber-AI Workforce Coalition to define how cybersecurity roles will evolve in the AI era.

For more information, visit the ISC2 AI Security Academy.

Napoli, fatturato da record: 384 milioni. Tutte le cifre tra incassi e spese del bilancio 2025/2026

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Chiude con un bilancio utile la SSC Napoli dopo la perdita registrata nell’esercizio precedente. Il ritorno in Champions League, il peso delle operazioni di mercato, il risarcimento ottenuto nell’ambito del contenzioso con IMG e lo sviluppo del comparto commerciale hanno permesso alla società partenopea di migliorare i propri risultati economici, a fronte di una crescita altrettanto significativa dei costi.

Stando ai documenti ufficiali, il Napoli ha archiviato l’esercizio al 30 giugno 2026 con un valore della produzione di 384,3 milioni di euro, rispetto ai circa 291 milioni dell’annata precedente. E’ un primato assoluto nella storia della società di Aurelio De Laurentiis, che supera anche i 359,2 milioni contabilizzati nel 2022/2023, stagione culminata con la conquista del terzo scudetto. Le entrate derivanti dai diritti televisivi hanno raggiunto quota 133 milioni di euro, contro i 78,1 milioni della stagione 2024/2025. Nello specifico, gli introiti provenienti dalle competizioni UEFA sono cresciuti da 3,9 a 49,5 milioni di euro, ai quali si aggiungono quelli legati alla partecipazione alla Supercoppa italiana. Le plusvalenze derivanti dalle cessioni dei calciatori si sono attestate intorno ai 102,7 milioni di euro. L’operazione più remunerativa è stata quella riguardante Victor Osimhen, che ha assicurato al club una plusvalenza di circa 73,2 milioni di euro. Tra le altre cessioni più significative ci sono quelle di Giacomo Raspadori, Alessandro Zanoli e Giovanni Simeone. I trasferimenti a titolo temporaneo hanno inoltre generato proventi per circa 8,7 milioni di euro. Nel dettaglio, ecco come si compongono i ricavi della SSC Napoli: Ricavi da gare: 31,3 milioni di euro (24,1 milioni nel 2024/2025); Ricavi da sponsorizzazioni: 52,9 milioni (48,1 milioni); Ricavi commerciali e royalties: 25,5 milioni (20,1 milioni); Proventi vari: 1,7 milioni (1,3 milioni); Proventi da cessione dei diritti televisivi: 133 milioni (78,1 milioni); Plusvalenze da cessione dei calciatori: 102,7 milioni (102,6 milioni); Altri ricavi e proventi diversi: 36,8 milioni (16,6 milioni); Contributi in conto esercizio: 500mila euro (0 euro); Totale: 384,3 milioni (290,9 milioni). Nella voce relativa agli altri proventi rientra anche un’entrata straordinaria derivante dall’accordo transattivo raggiunto dalla Lega Serie A nell’ambito della controversia con IMG per i diritti audiovisivi internazionali. L’intesa ha garantito alla società azzurra un introito di 19,3 milioni di euro.

Nell’esercizio chiuso al 30 giugno 2022, il Napoli aveva contabilizzato 12,1 milioni di euro di ricavi da gara e ulteriori 37,2 milioni provenienti da sponsorizzazioni, attività commerciali e royalties, per un totale di 49,3 milioni. Nella stagione 2025/2026, queste stesse voci hanno raggiunto complessivamente 109,7 milioni di euro, di cui: 31,3 milioni derivanti dal matchday, 52,9 milioni dalle sponsorizzazioni e 25,5 milioni dalle attività commerciali e dalle royalties.

Aumentano anche i costi di gestione. I costi della produzione hanno raggiunto quota 373,3 milioni di euro, rispetto ai 315,5 milioni dell’esercizio precedente, con una crescita di circa il 18%. Di seguito il dettaglio delle principali voci di costo del bilancio azzurro:

-Materie prime, sussidiarie, di consumo e merci: 10,4 milioni di euro (6,5 milioni nel 2024/2025);

-Costi per servizi: 30,5 milioni (36 milioni); Costi per godimento di beni di terzi: 8,1 milioni (6,7 milioni);

-Costi per il personale: 172,3 milioni (142,1 milioni), di cui 165,3 milioni per salari e stipendi (135,8 milioni);

-Ammortamenti e svalutazioni: 135 milioni (118,2 milioni);

-Variazione delle rimanenze: -800mila euro (-100mila euro);

-Oneri diversi di gestione: 17,7 milioni (6,2 milioni); Totale: 373,3 milioni (315,5 milioni).

A incidere maggiormente sull’aumento delle spese è stato il costo della squadra, anche in delle virtù operazioni sul mercato effettuate per soddisfare le richieste dell’ex allenatore Antonio Conte. Infatti, la voce salari è cresciuta dai 132,9 milioni dell’esercizio precedente ai 161,9 milioni contabilizzati al 30 giugno 2026. I soli compensi contrattuali dei calciatori hanno raggiunto quota 128,9 milioni di euro, ai quali si aggiungono i premi legati al conseguimento dei risultati sportivi, anch’essi in aumento.

Tuttavia, la crescita del fatturato ha permesso al Napoli di tornare a registrare un risultato economico positivo. La differenza tra il valore ei costi della produzione si è attestata intorno agli 11 milioni di euro, invertendo la tendenza negativa dell’esercizio precedente. Al netto della componente fiscale, il Napoli ha archiviato il bilancio al 30 giugno 2026 con un utile di circa 2,2 milioni di euro. Un risultato in netto miglioramento rispetto alla perdita di 21,4 milioni di euro registrata dodici mesi prima. Il club partenopeo torna quindi in utile dopo un solo anno caratterizzato da un risultato economico negativo, raggiungendo contestualmente il fatturato più elevato della propria storia.

Infine, sul documento contabile ci sono indicazioni significative anche sulla situazione patrimoniale e finanziaria della SSC Napoli. Il patrimonio netto si mantiene ampiamente positivo, attestandosi a circa 192,4 milioni di euro, mentre le disponibilità liquide ammontano a 172,9 milioni.

US Could ‘100%’ Lose Crypto Race To China, Digital Chamber CEO Warns

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

The crypto industry’s run of friendly treatment in Washington looks set to end. Cody Carbone, chief executive of The Digital Chamber, warned that a Democrat-controlled Congress will be “very hostile” to digital assets. Broad market structure legislation such as the CLARITY Act is now unlikely to pass.

Lawmakers are instead expected to focus on investigating crypto companies with ties to President Donald Trump. The political shift comes as Bitcoin trades at its lowest level since July.

Carbone said in an interview that the industry is unlikely to get the broad legislation it had pushed for. The CLARITY Act, meant to set clear rules for how digital assets are regulated in the US, has been left in limbo.

Two longtime critics of the industry are set to gain influence over financial policy: Senator Elizabeth Warren in the Senate and Representative Maxine Waters in the House.

For crypto firms, the agenda in Washington is moving from passing rules to defending against scrutiny.

Trump’s Crypto Ties Move Into the Spotlight

The industry is preparing for sweeping inquiries into crypto businesses connected to the President. Carbone expects Warren and Waters to press hard on Trump’s links to digital assets.

The Digital Chamber’s plan is to counter those investigations by shifting the debate to the technology itself. Carbone wants supportive lawmakers to steer hearings toward topics such as tokenization, so the conversation is about substance rather than politics.

The strategy depends on finding allies inside the incoming majority. Carbone admitted that pro-crypto Democrats are “few and far between,” but said their numbers are growing.

Many of the newly elected Democrats are young and focused on technology. According to Carbone, several have told the industry they are “crypto curious.”

The Digital Chamber plans to target moderates and junior committee members. Carbone said no single Democrat can take on Warren alone, but a larger group could. The aim is to build enough support within the party to push back against the more hostile voices.

Opposition to data centers poses another major threat, Carbone said. Local resistance to energy-heavy facilities hits both crypto mining and artificial intelligence.

Carbone said voters made their concerns clear in the November election and have a right to be skeptical. Still, the industry argues that bans and moratoriums are the wrong response.

The Digital Chamber plans to show lawmakers how data centers can bring jobs and revenue to struggling districts. Carbone described blockchain, digital assets and AI as closely linked industries, and framed leadership in all three as a matter of national competitiveness.

The big warning was about America’s global position. Asked whether the US could lose its lead in the sector within the next two years, Carbone answered and said “100%.”

Developers in crypto and AI are already moving to friendlier places such as the UAE and Singapore. Carbone pointed to China, which holds the most blockchain patents worldwide, as the country most likely to take the lead if the US falls behind.

“If we don’t lead, other adversaries will,” Carbone said, arguing that the US must set the global rules before its rivals do.

Nvidia Earnings Could Decide the Next Move for AI Stocks: 3 Things to Watch

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
