Nvidia earnings scheduled on November 17, with Wall Street expecting approximately $109 billion in quarterly revenue and $2.47 in adjusted EPS.

Data Center revenue is expected to approach $102 billion as demand for Blackwell GPUs and AI infrastructure remains strong.

Investors will focus on forward guidance, profit margins and leadership uncertainty to determine whether Nvidia can restore confidence across AI stocks.

Nvidia earnings could determine whether Wall Street’s AI trade stages a recovery or faces another wave of selling. The chipmaker is preparing to report its fiscal third-quarter 2027 results on November 17, with analysts expecting revenue of nearly $109 billion. However, strong financial results may not be enough to satisfy investors. Concerns surrounding AI spending, rising memory costs and leadership uncertainty have increased the pressure on the company. With Nvidia shares facing renewed volatility, can its latest earnings restore confidence in AI stocks, or is Wall Street preparing for another sell-off?

Nvidia faces exceptionally high expectations heading into its latest earnings report. Wall Street analysts expect the company to generate approximately $108.85 billion in revenue and $2.47 in adjusted earnings per share for fiscal Q3 2027. That would represent revenue growth of roughly 91% from $57.01 billion reported in the same quarter last year.

The company had previously guided for third-quarter revenue of $108 billion, plus or minus 2%, putting its official forecast broadly in line with analyst expectations. The previous quarter provides a strong benchmark. Nvidia reported $96.2 billion in Q2 FY2027 revenue, up 106% year over year, while adjusted earnings reached $2.22 per share. Its Data Center business generated $89 billion, accounting for the overwhelming majority of quarterly revenue.

Nvidia’s Data Center business remains the biggest driver of its valuation, making this segment the most important part of the upcoming earnings report. Analyst estimates put fiscal Q3 Data Center revenue at approximately $101.8 billion. Demand for Blackwell GPUs and expanding AI infrastructure investments from cloud providers are expected to support the increase.

Investors will also examine the company’s Vera Rubin platform, which is expected to become an increasingly important contributor to future revenue. Nvidia has said that Vera Rubin is entering production, while its earlier guidance indicated that the platform could account for around 20% of Data Center revenue in Q3.

The bigger question is whether hyperscalers and AI companies will continue spending aggressively on computing capacity. Microsoft, Amazon, Google and other major technology companies remain important customers in the AI infrastructure cycle.

If Nvidia signals that demand remains strong into fiscal 2028, it could support sentiment across the semiconductor sector. Any indication of slower orders or delayed infrastructure spending, however, could pressure not only NVDA but also AMD, Broadcom and other AI-linked stocks.

Nvidia’s earnings call will face another important test: whether rising costs and business uncertainty are beginning to affect its growth outlook. The company previously guided for a non-GAAP gross margin of approximately 74% in fiscal Q3. However, management also warned that higher memory costs could push margins lower in the following quarter, with a projected range of 71%–72% for Q4 FY2027.

China exposure is another issue investors will monitor. Nvidia’s previous Q3 revenue outlook did not assume any Data Center compute revenue from China, leaving investors to assess how much additional growth could come from other markets.

Leadership uncertainty adds another layer to the earnings event. Recent reports surrounding Jensen Huang’s departure and the company’s leadership transition have increased investor concerns ahead of the results. Interim leadership will need to reassure investors about product execution, customer relationships and the company’s long-term strategy.

The earnings call could therefore matter as much as the financial numbers. Investors will be looking for clear answers on future demand, margins and management’s confidence in the AI growth cycle.

Nvidia stock is approaching a decisive earnings test after losing nearly one-third of its value from its October record high. NVDA stock is trading around $159.92 on November 16, down approximately 32% from the October 6 peak of $243.37. The recent decline has brought the stock close to its 52-week low of $148.80.

NVIDIA $NVDA REPORTS EARNINGS TOMORROW AFTER THE STOCK MARKETS CLOSEHow do you think Nvidia's earnings go … do you think we see NVDA at $200 or $120 first? https://t.co/WUkct581pw pic.twitter.com/PhsNmOHrjS

The earnings reaction will likely determine which direction NVDA takes next. Strong results, upbeat Data Center guidance and confidence from interim CEO Colette Kress could help buyers regain control. However, weaker forward guidance or concerns about AI spending could push shares back toward recent lows.

Tesla confirms €3B Cybercab factory at Giga Berlin for up to 1M a year

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Tesla is going to build the Cybercab in Germany. The company confirmed today a first €3 billion investment in a new production building at Giga Berlin designed for up to 1 million Cybercabs a year, with production planned for 2029.

That’s a lot of robotaxis for a continent where Tesla can’t operate a single one. Under current EU rules, a driverless vehicle model is capped at 1,500 registrations a year.

Plant manager André Thierig announced the decision on X after Tesla told employees on Monday:

If you ever wondered, what we would be doing at Giga Berlin with all the land that we have… We are expanding! Cybercab production is coming to Giga Berlin! Today we announced a first investment of around € 3b in a new manufacturing building with an anticpated start of… pic.twitter.com/vpcDdxoqlW

Per Tesla’s statement, the €3 billion pays for the new production hall north of the existing plant, plus ancillary buildings and a second wastewater treatment plant. Tesla plans to file the building application before the end of the year, break ground in the first half of 2027, and start production in 2029. The company says it will be “the technologically most advanced Tesla factory in the world.”

It leaked a few hours earlier. Steven Scholtysek, an automation engineer at the plant, posted that Elon Musk personally signed off on the project last week and that the new hall will be bigger than the original factory.

The building isn’t new. Tesla laid out a plan to take Giga Berlin to 1 million vehicles a year in its 2023 permit filings and has been clearing land for it since. What’s new is the product. Until today, Tesla had only said the second hall wouldn’t be another Model Y factory.

Giga Berlin still builds only the Model Y. Tesla has been hiring again at Giga Berlin this year as European demand came back, with a goal of 7,500 cars a week. Even at that pace, the plant would fall short of the 500,000 units a year Tesla promised when it opened in 2022.

Tesla is also adding 4680 battery cell production at the site, with 18 GWh of annual capacity planned.

So the new hall alone is rated for twice what the entire existing plant was supposed to build. And it comes after Musk tied the Berlin expansion to the plant’s works council election in March, warning workers against IG Metall’s influence.

The EU has a type-approval path for fully driverless vehicles, but it’s a “small series” scheme limited to 1,500 registrations per vehicle type per year across the whole EU. In March, the Commission lifted that cap for automated valet parking. Robotaxis are still stuck in the small-series track.

Tesla also has no approval anywhere in Europe for its software to drive without supervision. The Dutch RDW approved the supervised version, now called Tesla Assisted Driving, and nine other countries recognize that approval. It still requires a driver paying attention. The Cybercab has no steering wheel.

Rules can move in three years. The Commission has said it wants large-series approval for other driverless uses, and a UN regulation for automated driving systems is in the works. But as of today, Tesla is committing €3 billion to a vehicle it can’t sell in volume in Europe.

Tesla could export from Berlin, of course. But 1 million Cybercabs a year on top of Giga Texas implies a robotaxi business many times bigger than anything on the road today. Texas, where Cybercab production started in April, had 463 Cybercabs registered as of late October. And Tesla just opened personal Cybercab orders at $29,990 plus a mandatory $299 monthly subscription, usable only inside its Robotaxi geofences.

Tesla can’t even launch its driver-assist system in Europe under its own name. What it’s rolling out there isn’t “Full Self-Driving.” It’s “Tesla Assisted Driving,” a level 2 system that needs a driver watching the road at all times, and it took a long fight with regulators to get it approved in 10 countries.

The Cybercab is a much harder sell. No steering wheel, no pedals, so it has to be certified as a level 4 vehicle with nobody inside to take over. That’s a completely different approval from anything Tesla has gotten in Europe. As of this spring, the EU said no automaker had even used its existing driverless approval path.

And the US program doesn’t inspire confidence. Robotaxi is still confined to geofences, still off highways, and it keeps crashing: Tesla had reported 22 incidents to NHTSA by July, and our analysis of the data earlier this year put it at one crash every 57,000 miles, roughly 9 times worse than the average human driver, with a safety monitor on board. The only reason those crashes don’t get more attention is that they happen at low speed. Of course they do. Tesla keeps the cars on slow city streets. Put the same system on the autobahn and we’d expect more crashes, and worse ones.

This is Musk’s modus operandi for the last few years: bet on autonomy before it’s ready. Tesla removed the Cybercab’s steering wheel assuming the software would be solved by the time it mattered. It wasn’t, and now Tesla is putting €3 billion into a German factory on the same assumption.

Good for the workers in Grünheide, and maybe the hall can build something else if this doesn’t work out. But spending €3 billion to build up to 1 million steering-wheel-less cars a year for a market where Tesla can’t legally run a single one, based on a program that hasn’t left the geofence in Texas, is extremely risky.

Will there be one unsupervised Cybercab carrying paying passengers in Europe when the Grünheide line starts in 2029? We wouldn’t bet on it.

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

The first lesson you learn in Oral Sex 101: Giving a good blow job is an art, not a science.

As with any and every sex act, what turns on one person might not do it for another, but most folks on the receiving end of fellatio are just happy to be there. “[People] love blow jobs because they get to relax and receive and just enjoy what's happening in front of them,” says Vanessa Marin, LMFT, a sex therapist in Los Angeles. Nvidia and IBM’s 13,000x Quantum Speedup Raises New ‘Q-Day’ Questions for Bitcoin

📅 2026-11-16  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Nvidia, IBM and Fermilab announced new tools for quantum error correction on Nov. 16, and the news comes amid a debate in the crypto industry about "Q-Day," the moment when a quantum computer will be able to break Bitcoin's cryptography. As of Monday, the price of Bitcoin stands at $64,580.

Nvidia released a new decoding algorithm, TCBP-JOBS, on Nov. 16. According to the company, the algorithm delivers up to 13,000x faster decoding compared with existing leading algorithms, and it cuts the computation time for researching new quantum computing designs from 12 weeks to 45 minutes.

Nvidia said that the algorithm helps researchers find designs that use physical qubits more efficiently, "shortening the path to practical fault-tolerant quantum computing."

Nvidia and IBM also announced plans for a modular quantum computing codesign platform with Fermilab. IBM and Nvidia are placing Nvidia DGX B200 systems next to IBM quantum computers, including those powered by IBM Quantum Nighthawk, and researchers will use the platform to develop and test error-correction codes.

"Making quantum computers useful for science requires advances in error correction, applications and the systems that bring quantum processors and accelerated computing together," said Krysta Svore, vice president of applied research for quantum computing at Nvidia.

A 2022 study by researchers from the University of Sussex estimated that breaking Bitcoin's encryption within a day would require around 13 million physical qubits.

IBM Quantum Nighthawk, which IBM calls its fastest quantum processor, has 120 qubits. According to Nvidia, errors in quantum processors "limit the complexity and duration of the computations they can perform."

In 2024, Bitcoin developer Hunter Beast proposed BIP-360, a new output type that would protect coins from quantum attacks. In July 2025, Casa co-founder Jameson Lopp also suggested a plan to phase out legacy signatures and move coins to quantum-resistant addresses.

Early coins mined by Satoshi Nakamoto sit on addresses with public keys that are already visible on the blockchain.

In November 2024, NIST proposed to stop allowing vulnerable public-key algorithms, including elliptic curve cryptography, after 2035.

Trump leaves door open to Kim Jong Un joining high-stakes talks with Xi, Putin

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

President Donald Trump is set to meet with Chinese President Xi Jinping and Russian President Vladimir Putin during his Asia trip, and he left open the possibility that North Korean leader Kim Jong Un could join the high-stakes talks.

President Donald Trump offered a brief, unexpected response about North Korea during a White House gaggle Monday, adding a new wrinkle to his closely watched trip to Asia this week.

Trump suggested North Korean leader Kim Jong Un could make an appearance during his China trip, telling reporters, "We might have Kim Jong Un. He may come, I don’t know. That’s up to President Xi."

The comment came in response to a reporter's question about the upcoming high-stakes meetings between Trump, Chinese President Xi Jinping, and Russian President Vladimir Putin at the sidelines of the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation (APEC) Leaders' Meeting in Shenzhen, China, and whether the trio planned to discuss North Korean nuclear capabilities.

PRESIDENT TRUMP TO HOLD HISTORIC TRILATERAL SUMMIT WITH XI AND PUTIN

President Donald Trump is expected to meet with Chinese President Xi Jinping and Russian President Vladimir Putin Thursday. (Reuters)

Trump and Kim last met in 2019, when their Hanoi summit ended without an agreement on North Korea’s nuclear program. Nonetheless, Pyongyang has since expanded its nuclear and missile capabilities, while efforts to restart talks have stalled. In August, Trump said he expected to meet Kim "later this year," but North Korea pushed back, with Kim Yo Jong saying the leaders’ relationship remained "excellent" while accusing Washington of maintaining hostile policies toward Pyongyang, Fox News Digital reported at the time.

Meanwhile, a U.S. official said last week, according to ABC News, that there were no plans for North Korea engagement during Trump’s Asia trip, and experts say the country is unlikely to feature prominently in the trilateral talks.

South Korean news outlet Yonhap News Agency reported Monday that South Korea’s Foreign Ministry said it hoped high-level talks on the sidelines of the APEC summit would contribute to peace and stability on the Korean Peninsula. South Korean Foreign Minister Cho Hyun reportedly raised the possibility of renewed U.S.-North Korea dialogue at the summit with his Chinese counterpart, Wang Yi, in Beijing on Friday, stressing that South Korea has vital interests in Korean Peninsula issues and hopes that its perspective is taken into account.

NORTH KOREA MISSILE COULD PUT GUAM IN RANGE AS KIM REBUFFS TRUMP OUTREACH: EXPERT

President Donald Trump meets with North Korean leader Kim Jong Un at the North Korean side of the border at the village of Panmunjom in Demilitarized Zone, on June 30, 2019. (Susan Walsh/AP)

The South Korean news agency also reported Monday that Andrew Yeo, a senior fellow at the U.S.-based Brookings Institution, said North Korea’s nuclear program is unlikely to be discussed at the summit, citing differences between Washington and Moscow over Pyongyang’s nuclear ambitions. The report also highlighted comments from Korea Economic Institute senior fellow Ellen Kim, who reportedly suggested the leaders could briefly discuss North Korea behind closed doors, but nuclear arms control and the war in Iran are likely to take priority. Ellen Kim added that Xi or Putin could relay a message from Trump to Kim Jong Un, though they may be reluctant to support U.S.-North Korea negotiations for fear of losing influence over Pyongyang.

The trilateral meeting is scheduled for 2:30 p.m. Thursday local time at Shenzhen Kylin Villa, according to Trump’s public schedule.

White House spokeswoman Olivia Wales said Friday that Trump planned to discuss nuclear arms reduction with Xi and Putin, while a senior U.S. official said Iran would also be on the agenda. The Associated Press reported Monday that Trump plans to raise Russia’s war in Ukraine at the meeting as well.

Russian President Vladimir Putin greets Chinese President Xi Jinping as Foreign Minister Sergei Lavrov, second from right, and First Deputy Prime Minister Igor Shuvalov, right, look on during their meeting in the Grand Kremlin Palace in Moscow on May 8, 2015. (Sasha Mordovets/Getty Images)

Asked Monday what it would take for Putin to back down from the war in Ukraine, Trump said he thinks Putin "is backing down a little bit."

"I think he's backing down a little bit. I think he could be tougher," Trump told reporters Monday outside the White House. "I think it could be a lot tougher, frankly."

Fox News Digital has reached out to the White House for additional comment.

What do you mean bad actors I wasn’t doing that much bad things pic.twitter.com/swHfeOLTuVNovember 16, 2026

Bitly, the URL shortener website, got in hot water this week as users (or 'bad actors' as the company calls them) found out they could hijack old broken links and put whatever they like in their place. As you can imagine, chaos ensued.

Many of the links have since been fixed, but it seems like the McDonald's Twitter account once accidentally said the Swedish McDonald's team are responsible for 9/11,, and KFC linked to the Mewgenics wiki page for Steven, an NPC from the game. McDonald's was caught multiple times in this hijacking, with the Canadian X account hijacked to link the We Are Charlie Kirk song.

Though it's hard to verify now, it also seems like the Windows X page accidentally linked to the "r/FuckMicrosoft" subreddit too.

Big brands weren't the only ones caught in the mass hijacking. President Donald Trump seemingly tweeted a lot of Bitly links back in 2011, which were hijacked to instead link to a 'Trollface' meme song, a picture of a soggy cat titled "Soggy cat", and a link to the Epstein files Wikipedia page.

images for archival purposes pic.twitter.com/fRRr8LMGeENovember 15, 2026

A day after this exploit started to make its way around X, Bitly shared that it had identified the issue and said, "We have suspended the accounts involved and are disabling the affected links. Our Trust and Safety team is working to address the issue and strengthen protections against similar misuse in the future."

It seems like the links in question were broken before being hijacked, and users simply managed to redirect those links to whatever site they wanted. However, in Bitly's documentation, it says the "back-halves used on these links cannot be re-used — whether they're automatically-generated or custom back-halves." So it's not entirely clear how these users manage to use links when Bitly says they supposedly cannot be reused.

Earlier today, we identified an issue that allowed bad actors to repurpose certain old, inactive links to redirect people to content unrelated to the original posts. We have suspended the accounts involved and are disabling the affected links. Our Trust and Safety team is working…November 15, 2026

In the grand scheme, brands linking to various songs and Wikipedia articles are relatively harmless, but the ability to hijack links does pose a problem: what if hackers could do the same with a link to something containing malware? As such, it's probably a good reminder to Bitly and any other URL shortener websites to double-check that users can't reuse old broken links, as who knows who could have possibly made them first.

It was not until 2019, after just finishing a degree in law and media, that they decided to throw out the last few years of education, build their PC, and start writing about gaming instead. In that time, he has covered the latest doodads, contraptions, and gismos, and loved every second of it. Hey, it’s better than writing case briefs.

Anthony Boyle Plays the Ultimate Boy Wonder Tech Bro in The Altruists

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Before Anthony Boyle and I begin our interview, we’ve already covered a fair amount of ground. Serendipitously, we’ve ridden up alongside each other on our bicycles. Boyle is still acquainting himself with the city’s two-wheeled possibilities; he’s a recent but enthusiastic convert. “It opens up the world!” he declares.

Our first stop is a tiny gallery in east London, where an exhibition of contemporary Irish art is on display. Boyle, a Belfast transplant, has lived in London off and on for the past few years. “Is that the Big Fish? It’s surely the Big Fish,” Boyle asks of one painting, invoking the giant salmon sculpture that sits on the waterfront in Belfast. The actor is wearing jeans, low-profile sneakers, and a blue T-shirt with a horse printed on the back, a recent impulse purchase—it reminds him of childhood summer vacations spent riding in Donegal.

Recently, Boyle, 32, has established himself with his shrewd portrayal of IRA commander Brendan “The Dark” Hughes in the TV series Say Nothing and his assured turn as the flamboyant Arthur Guinness in Steven Knight’s House of Guinness. Earlier this year, the film I See Buildings Fall Like Lightning—in which Boyle plays a college-educated food-delivery cyclist—premiered to impassioned reviews at Cannes. “The film feels like a socialist war cry,” Boyle says, describing its portrayal of working-class communities in modern Britain.

And this November, he took another exhilarating left turn, starring opposite Julia Garner in Netflix’s The Altruists, an eight-part series about the spectacular collapse of the cryptocurrency exchange FTX—and one of a handful of new films and TV series that spin our recent techno-history into entertainment. Boyle plays the company’s disgraced founder, Sam Bankman-Fried, whose dizzying ascent and precipitous downfall have made him a hubristic emblem of the crypto age. The show casts Bankman-Fried and his on-again, off-again girlfriend and colleague Caroline Ellison (Garner) as hyperintelligent idealists, at once fiercely competitive and committed to “effective altruism”—a philosophical movement that seeks to optimize charitable giving.

The awkward physicality, unruly hair, and peculiar vocal inflections that Boyle brought to the role are a world away from the steely conviction of an IRA soldier or the patrician swagger of a Guinness. For his first meeting with The Altruists’ creators, he went to the budget retailer Primark, bought a black sweater, smeared it with Cup Noodle stains, and mussed his hair. He logged onto Zoom in character, improvising that he had Mark Zuckerberg waiting on another call. His childhood friend and Say Nothing costar Lola Petticrew, with whom he rehearsed, offered invaluable feedback. “When you do that voice, I want to throw a cup of tea over you,” Boyle remembers Petticrew saying. He was delighted. “There’s an inner conflict in him that makes his performances unpredictable,” says Garner. “Whatever he’s thinking or feeling, you can see it in his eyes. You can’t teach someone to have those eyes.”

Once he was offered the role, Boyle watched footage of Bankman-Fried, fascinated by an awkwardness at odds with his extraordinary influence. He even corresponded with Bankman-Fried in prison: “This is the smartest person I’ve ever been in contact with,” he says of their exchange. “People don’t think they’re doing evil,” Boyle says, reflecting on Bankman-Fried’s motivations. “They believe they’re doing things for the greater good.”

PARALLEL LINES Anthony Boyle wears a Dries Van Noten sweater, JW Anderson jeans, and Paul Smith belt.

As a boy in West Belfast, Boyle trained at the Royal Welsh College of Music & Drama before his onstage breakout at age 21 as Scorpius Malfoy in Harry Potter and the Cursed Child, which earned him an Olivier Award. “Before that, my longest play was a five-day run at a community center,” he says. In addition to his striking Say Nothing and House of Guinness performances, Boyle’s career has encompassed roles in the World War II drama Masters of the Air and the John Wilkes Booth–inspired series Manhunt. “I met with a bunch of cowboys,” he says of that role, “it took me a while to lose the habit of picking up random things, chewing, spitting them out.” He delights in the contrasts in his work: “I’m always trying to do what I haven’t done,” he says. As we walk through the park, a few men practicing martial arts catch Boyle’s attention, and he acquires a pair of nunchucks, giving them a whirl with enough confidence that I can’t discern whether this is a genuine skill or a gloriously committed bluff. “Pretty good, right?” he asks.

Since The Altruists finished shooting last November, Boyle completed the forthcoming dark comedy The Worst (with Keira Knightley and Alicia Vikander) and has been filming Charmer, a new series in which he plays a professional seducer hired to test the fidelity of his wealthy clients’ partners. Next comes a second season of House of Guinness, with just two days off between filming. “I need to find little bits of joy and craic where I can,” he says.

One of those joys is returning home. Boyle has lived in London and New York and recently moved back to Belfast, although his schedule means he seldom spends much time there. Recently at a McDonald’s with his cousin, Boyle’s order was supersized without any prompting. “Anything for The Dark,” the server said—a reference to his Say Nothing role, and, Boyle reckons, just about the coolest thing that could have happened on his home turf. He’s been honored by his hometown in additional ways: Belfast artist Colin Davidson recently painted his portrait and unveiled it at the city’s Lyric Theatre. Davidson offered him some sage advice while the actor was sitting for him: “He said, ‘Never soften your edges, Anthony.’ ”

“Coming from where we come from, this career seemed a world away, but seeing him chase it made it feel more tangible for me,” says Petticrew. Working together on Say Nothing, Petticrew adds, was meaningful beyond the acting: “To reflect the place we both love so deeply and tell its story was probably one of the most important things we’ll ever do.”

An upcoming project will bring Boyle even nearer to his origins. In a four-part adaptation of Michael Magee’s acclaimed 2023 novel Close to Home, he plays Sean, a young man returning to a Belfast reckoning with the aftermath of the sectarian violence of the Troubles. Boyle attended the same school as Magee a few years apart, and the novel depicts a world Boyle recognizes—down to the hardscrabble streets and poorly paid nightclub jobs. “I felt like someone was reaching out of the book and grabbing me,” he says. “It articulated thoughts and experiences I thought only I had.” Boyle urged the book on everyone around him. “I bought a hundred copies,” he says—one Christmas, he distributed 10 among his family. “None of them opened it.” Television, he realized, might be a way to get the story into their lives.

Playing Sean demanded a new methodology from Boyle: “It was almost coming back to myself in a way and not performing,” he says. Boyle, who is also executive producing the series, championed local talent and developed it closely with Magee, running scenes with him until the dialogue felt lived-in. “I wanted to make Close to Home for people in the areas I grew up in.”

We’re wrapping up our walk when he tells me he is meeting his father to help his younger sister move apartments. She recently arrived in London for college and was temporarily living in his house with a friend while looking for a place of her own. Boyle would leave for work before dawn and return late to find them blasting Sinéad O’Connor and dancing in the kitchen. “It was infectious,” he says. We’re now in front of Hackney City Farm, a cloistered urban oasis with livestock and gardens. His new T-shirt has reminded him how much he misses riding, and he wants to know where in London he can get on a horse. When I mention that the farm has donkeys, he’s thrilled: “I love a donkey.”

A week later, I run into Boyle again, shouting to me as I bike past. This time he’s accompanied by his older sibling and their partner. He’s showing them around before heading to New York for The Altruists press tour. Swaggering along the road, he is just as buoyant as before, taking himself off on another adventurous detour.

In this story: Grooming, Liz Taw; tailor, Nafisa Tosh.

‘The only question is if it’s a soft recession or something more dramatic’: Gary Gensler warns the AI bubble is likely to burst and result in a recession before November 2028

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

The Former Securities and Exchange Commission Chair makes a scary prediction

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

There seems to be a slew of warning signs pointing to the inevitability of the AI bubble bursting a lot sooner than most people think.

The controversial exit of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang (who happens to be recognized as the "high priest" of the global AI buildout) and the stock market volatility tied to companies that have made huge investments in AI are two of the bigger signs pointing to the AI bubble bursting. Plus, there have been plenty of major personalities in the tech and business worlds who have given their thoughts on it becoming a reality at some point.

One of those individuals is the Former Securities and Exchange Commission Chair, Gary Gensler, who sat down with CNN to give his own take on the possibility of the AI burst event coming to fruition.

Here’s what he had to say and why his prediction places it near the next presidential election.

Gensler, who ran the Securities and Exchange Commission from 2021 until 2025, told CNN that today’s AI infrastructure buildout has already crossed into bubble territory and will likely lead into a recession before the next presidential election. "It's already a bubble," the former Wall Street regulator stated. "The only question is if it's a soft recession or something more dramatic."

On the topic of all the big spending dedicated to strengthening the current AI infrastructure (which is said to have reached $800 billion a year by some estimates), Gensler pointed to past trends where major investments in technology eventually stagnate.

“History tells us that when you have a new technology and a significant investment ... you come to a point where it plateaus. It retrenches,” Gensler noted. “The only question is if it’s a soft recession or something more dramatic.” The previous instances he cited as big tech investments that eventually met steep declines included railroads, 1920s electricity and the 1990s dot-com boom.

Sign up to the Tom's AI Guide weekly newsletter summing up all the biggest AI news you need to know. Plus, analysis from our AI editors and tips on how to use the latest AI tools!

As for when he sees the AI bubble bursting, he points to it going down sometime before the next presidential election. "I think we're going to have this repricing and a softening of the economy that happens before November 2028," he went on to say. "I can't say exactly when and how stormy, but I would be battening down some hatches."

Gensler is among the many other voices in the tech and investment industries who have given their thoughts on the possibilities of the AI bubble bursting and the resulting fallout. Gary Marcus, the former head of AI for Uber, argued to the Financial Times that today’s AI boom is a massive financial bubble driven by unsustainable economics and technological dead ends:

“If there is a deflation of the AI bubble, the optimists say that the new infrastructure will remain even if the companies do not — just as railways survived the 19th-century railway bust. However, this fails to reckon with the reality of depreciation (few pieces of silicon hold their value for very long because better chips inevitably come along) and the possibility that LLMs could be displaced by more efficient models less dependent on massive numbers of expensive AI chips. In placing massive hyperscaling bets, investors are setting lavish expectations about future earnings. But LLMs are not likely to replicate the near monopolies that have made the market power of current tech giants hard to assail. A better analogy for them might be airlines, which are hobbled by small margins, intense competition, high expenses and dependence on hardware created by outside vendors.” – @garymarcus in @FinancialTimesJune 26, 2026

The Canadian mining financier Frank Giustra was quite frank with his AI bubble burst prediction while speaking at this year’s New Orleans Investment Conference. “There will be a reckoning, and some of these companies will get destroyed," he said. During that same conversation, investor Grant Williams compared the disconnect between recognizing the stock market dangers attached to AI investments and taking proper precautions to a natural disaster.

"If we told you a hurricane was heading to New Orleans, everybody would take precautions," he explained. "The interesting thing about stock market bubbles is people don't really think about what can go wrong. They're still thinking about making money."

While many voices are speaking on the very real possibility of an AI bubble burst, there are business bigwigs who have made it clear that everyone shouldn’t be all that worried about it. During a sit-down interview with CNBC, Amazon founder Jeff Bezos tried to ease everyone’s fears about such a financially devastating event coming to pass. “Even if it does turn out to be a bubble, you shouldn’t worry about it because the bubble is driving investment and a lot of the investment is going to turn out to be very healthy,” he proclaimed.

Bret Taylor, the chairman of OpenAI and CEO of Sierra, spoke to The Verge and flat-out acknowledged that an AI bubble exists while still noting that it's totally fine. And while speaking to CNBC at the 2026 World Economic Forum in Davos, Switzerland, his AI optimism pointed to a scenario where the market will correct itself as weaker industry players and overfunded ventures will fall by the wayside.

“When everyone knows that AI is going to have a huge impact on the economy across a huge range of industries and workflows, money is plentiful,” Taylor noted. “I think over the next few years, you’ll see a correction, you also see consolidation, but I don’t think you can get innovation without that kind of messy competition.”

Gensler’s prophetic words about the AI bubble bursting before we even reach the next presidential election are alarming, yes.

But I can envision a world where potential candidates use that moment as one of their political talking points in a bid to ease back on all that AI spending, put more into the American populace and hold back on bailing out any of the AI companies that are hurting from such a traumatic event.

All signs point to something devastating happening to the AI industry at some point, especially with the huge backlash against data centers and the politicians who are looking to put in moratoriums on their continued development.

Privacy on Solana is ‘non-negotiable’ for institutional adoption, Mert Mumtaz says

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The Helius CEO says financial firms need confidentiality onchain and argues Zcash’s rise could push bitcoin to improve.

Privacy features are a must for bringing institutional finance onto Solana, according to Mert Mumtaz, co-founder and CEO of Helius.

"Institutions will not come onchain unless they have privacy and compliance guarantees," Mumtaz told The Block on Monday at Solana Breakpoint in London.

Mumtaz, whose company builds Solana infrastructure, said confidential lending was a big example. Financial institutions need to be able to transact without exposing private information, and moving onchain shouldn't mean accepting an inferior product, he added.

He compared it to the internet's shift from HTTP to HTTPS, which added stronger protections for sensitive information to help scale online commerce. Blockchain privacy should follow a similar path, he argued.

"Crypto without privacy is not crypto," Mumtaz said. This also extends to Zcash, which Mumtaz sees as a store of value that complements bitcoin.

"If you derive a store of value from first principles, Zcash looks very close to the ideal solution," he said.

Despite acknowledging bitcoin's longer history and wider distribution, he sees competition between the two benefiting users.

"I think it’s good for Bitcoin that Zcash is now getting traction," he said, adding that it could put pressure on bitcoin to improve.

Mumtaz was also optimistic about crypto infrastructure security amid a wave of hacks and exploits, including the worst in history — a $6.4 billion theft from crypto exchange Binance last month. He said these attacks expose weaknesses that developers can repair.

"Within a year, I think crypto will actually be much more secure than the alternative," he said. "I recognize it’ll be painful a little bit, but fundamentally speaking, this is what we’re prepared for."

With only a few days to go before GTA 6 arrives, Sony has dropped the price of the DualSense Edge by $30

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Don't say that Sony can't time a discount well - the brand has finally given its DualSense Edge pro controller an early Black Friday deal, the week of GTA 6's release. I've seen previous Black Fridays, Prime Days, and other huge AAA releases happen, and the price of the DualSense Edge hasn't budged, but as soon as it's being considered one of the best controllers for GTA 6, the discounts finally arrive.

If you're a Prime member and you order this today, there's a good chance it'll arrive with you before the 19th, when the game becomes available to play. It's a whole $30 off the cost of the gamepad, which brings the $200 price tag to a much more palatable $169 at Amazon.

The discount stands for both the Black and White models of the DualSense Edge, and the price is the same at Walmart if you want to try your luck at collecting it at a nearby store before the 19th. In the UK, the price has come down to £149.99.

The last time we saw the DualSense Edge drop to this price was back in June, but its lowest price ever is $159.99, only $10 more affordable than it is now. According to the Amazon price history data I'm seeing, it's dropped as low as it is currently only three or four times since its arrival on the shelves in 2023. Before May last year, it had only dipped its toe in the discount aisle down to $200 or even $195 - which were sorry excuses for discounts.

Personally, seeing as Rockstar won't launch GTA 6 on PC at the same time as consoles, the DualSense Edge is the controller I'd recommend experiencing the game with if you have the budget. I said a similar thing last week when I recommended other gamepads for GTA 6, but seeing as Rockstar has been in cahoots with PlayStation for launch, there are all sorts of DualSense perks you can enjoy in the game. The DualSense Edge will get access to all of those PlayStation-bespoke features, but give you more comfort and functionality than the stock controller (which is also discounted at the moment, FYI).

What are you actually getting? Besides the hold-all charging case the Edge comes in, you also get some swappable thumbsticks, two magnetic back paddles (that come in various shapes), and refined, rounded corners to the bottoms of the grips. These make a genuine difference to how comfortable the DualSense is in your hands, and trigger stop switches give you the chance to find the squeeze that feels best for you.

There's more you can do with the DualSense on the software side, and clever profile-switching buttons rest below the thumbsticks. In a practical sense, I think most players will set these up and not touch them often, so the paragraph above lists the stuff you'll actually care about when playing GTA.

What you're not getting here is any sort of enhanced battery life - so don't expect that to be any less dire than the regular DualSense. Similarly, the Edge still uses potentiometer thumbstick modules, which means they're just as prone to stick drift from prolonged use. That's a real shame, but at least you'll have access to haptic feedback and adaptive trigger resistance, which the other best PS5 candidates don't have due to licensing issues.

Kava moves to shut down lending platform after hackers drain nearly $4 million

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

HARD Lend plans to permanently disable new borrowing after attackers stole 53.9 million KAVA, leaving questions over how depositors will recover their funds.

Kava plans to retire HARD Lend's borrowing functionality after hackers stole 53.9 million KAVA, leaving the lending protocol facing a substantial liquidity shortfall.

The blockchain network confirmed on Nov. 16 that the Nov. 14 attack exploited a vulnerability in HARD Lend's accounting system, allowing attackers to withdraw existing KAVA tokens through manipulated collateral and liquidation transactions.

The stolen tokens were worth $3.7 million before the subsequent market selloff, according to blockchain analytics firm Bitquery.

Kava said it had patched the vulnerability and introduced safeguards to prevent attackers from repeating the exploit. It also accelerated plans to retire the lending service, with a governance proposal expected to permanently disable new lending and borrowing.

The proposal must pass a seven-day governance voting process, while existing protective measures remain in force.

“The planned retirement of HARD Lend's legacy lending functionality is being accelerated,” Kava said, adding that it would address remaining positions through an orderly process.

The company emphasized that the breach was confined to HARD Lend and did not compromise the underlying Kava blockchain or create additional tokens.

However, the incident has raised questions about how the protocol will settle outstanding obligations after losing almost all the tokens available in its lending pool.

Kava said attackers manipulated the value of BUSD collateral within HARD Lend, allowing them to repeatedly borrow KAVA and trigger liquidations that cleared their debts without recovering the borrowed tokens.

An investigation by blockchain analysis firm Bitquery found that the attackers borrowed about 55.2 million KAVA across 127 transactions and retained 53.9 million tokens after repayments involving other borrowers.

The attackers subsequently moved about 49.2 million KAVA to BNB Chain through LayerZero.

Bitquery traced the sale of 48.4 million KAVA, which generated approximately $2.09 million in BNB and USDT.

The investigation also identified a failed transaction involving 612,416 USDT through the Relay bridge. The service rejected the transfer with a “BLOCKED_WALLET” message, preventing the requested payout to Ethereum.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

November 14 evidence describes three distinct access paths: lender claims, a failed bridge order and exchange transfers.

Lender withdrawals require usable KAVA in the pool. Blocking a bridge payout or reopening exchange transfers does not itself supply that cash.

Pool and bridge observations are dated November 14. Relay record was read at 16:20 UTC; BNB Chain/Ethereum data end at 16:08 UTC. The withdrawable flag was untested. Coinone notice is not a live status feed.

Select dated reported balances and access states. Connectors depict access routes only, not quantitative flows. Pool outline is schematic, not proportional. No derived totals, valuations or exchange conversions.

Pool and bridge observations are dated November 14. Relay record was read at 16:20 UTC; BNB Chain/Ethereum data end at 16:08 UTC. The withdrawable flag was untested. Coinone notice is not a live status feed.

Kava separately confirmed that part of the attackers' funds had been frozen and said it was working with exchanges, ecosystem partners, and law enforcement to trace and recover the remaining assets.

However, it did not disclose how much had been secured or whether the frozen funds included the USDT identified by Bitquery.

The recovery efforts have yet to resolve the more immediate problem facing HARD Lend's depositors.

Bitquery's Nov. 14 reconstruction showed that the lending pool owed users approximately 55.2 million KAVA while holding just 0.19 KAVA available for withdrawals.

The blockchain network said “Individual user deposits were largely unaffected,” but did not explain how that assessment relates to outstanding lender claims or depleted pool liquidity. It also promised further guidance for affected users as the retirement proposal progresses.

All 5 Versions Of Dungeon Crawler Carl, Ranked

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Dungeon Crawler Carl fans are waiting patiently for an update from author Matt Dinniman regarding Book 9, currently using the working title The Beautiful Place and expected to release in two massive volumes in the coming year or so. However, in the meantime, readers are filling their time going back through for a thorough re-read, which makes sense given how sprawling and somewhat complicated the story has become by the end of Book 8, A Parade of Horribles.

Fortunately, fans have a handful of ways to enjoy Dinniman's incredible story in addition to the original books themselves, including Jeff Hays' acclaimed audiobooks on Audible, the more dramatized Audio Immersion Tunnel experience, the WEBTOON and its print adaptations, as well as the newly released Dungeon Crawler Carl RPG.

Whether you're a newcomer to the series or preparing for a second (or third, or fourth) re-read ahead of Book 9, here are the best ways to experience the World Dungeon.

The Dungeon Crawler Carl RPG from Renegade Games Studios has already started arriving in players' hands who decided to back the crowdfunding campaign earlier this year, which hit a record-breaking $13 million in funding on Backerkit. The game is set to arrive in retail later this month, and it includes a unique adaptation of the first five floors of Carl and Donut's journey through the World Dungeon as a pre-made campaign.

That said, early reception among backers has been quite mixed so far, with players expressing concern over the quality of the product, a massively unorganized structure to the core RPG books, a half-baked gameplay system that could've used more time with play-testing, etc.

Beyond those issues, the RPG doesn't really serve as a complete Dungeon Crawler Carl experience, and it's hard to imagine anyone diving in without having read the original books already. As such, it's definitely not the primary way anyone interested in Dinniman's story should experience things, but it's a great option for veteran fans looking to immerse themselves in the universe in a new way.

If you haven't had a chance to check out the Dungeon Crawler Carl WEBTOON comics yet, they're easily the best visual adaptation of the series so far. With an official television series in the works, that's likely to change in the future, but for the time being, these are well worth checking out.

Launched in July 2025, the WEBTOON adaptation is published by Aethon Webcomics, adapted by writer Tevagah, and illustrated by Laurel Pursuit Studios, with Matt Dinniman himself consulted throughout the process. The full-color, vertically scrolling comic brings Carl and Princess Donut's adventures to life with vibrant artwork, over-the-top action sequences, and some fantastic interpretations of the books' most memorable characters and monsters.

However, while the WEBTOON series offers a great alternative option, not everyone is a fan of the anime-style artwork, and the series is also largely incomplete. So far, there are only two collected print volumes of the comic in circulation, covering the first 30 episodes and Carl and Donut's journey through the dungeon's first floor and toward the second. Meanwhile, the WEBTOON itself has only just finished adapting the original novel, with its early-access chapters reaching the end of the second floor, and including an exclusive epilogue.

The Dungeon Crawler Carl Audio Immersion Tunnel on Soundbooth Theater is arguably the most unique adaptation of the original story on offer. Unlike the traditional audiobooks, this production transforms Dinniman's novels into a fully realized audio drama, complete with a full cast of voice actors, original music, cinematic sound effects, and even additional scenes and bonus material approved by Dinniman himself.

Jeff Hays reprises his iconic roles as Carl, Princess Donut, and the dungeon's sadistic AI, joined by a talented cast that includes Andrea Parsneau, Travis Baldree, and Heath Miller. The result is something closer to a television series without the visuals, giving familiar moments from the books an entirely new level of immersion. So far, three complete seasons have been released, each adapting one of the original novels. Season 1 covers Dungeon Crawler Carl and the first two dungeon floors, Season 2 tackles Carl's Doomsday Scenario and the third floor, while Season 3 adapts The Dungeon Anarchist's Cookbook and its infamous Iron Tangle.

The third season wrapped up in June 2026, but there's still a long way to go, unfortunately. With eight books in the original series already published and only three adapted so far, it could be several more years before the Audio Immersion Tunnel catches up. Nevertheless, for fans looking to revisit Carl and Donut's adventures in a completely different way, it's easily one of the best options available.

Honestly, Jeff Hays' Audible versions of the Dungeon Crawler Carl series are almost as good as the original books themselves, and some would argue they're even better. Hays is equipped with an impressive range of voices, becoming inseparable from Princess Donut's voice to the point where he's even been cast for her role in the television adaptation. Beyond Donut, his distinctive interpretation of each character throughout the series truly brings the books to life.

In fact, there are certain moments in Hays' performance that take the text on the page to a completely different level and give listeners something that's not possible from just reading the books. One moment that comes to mind is Princess Donut's performance of "All Eyes On Me" in The Butcher's Masquerade, which on paper is a somewhat emotional moment but comes off more comedic than anything, whereas Hays actually sings the song and it's extremely powerful and has even brought some listeners to tears given the context.

Obviously, it's hard to beat Matt Dinniman's original novels when it comes to getting the definitive Dungeon Crawler Carl experience. What started as a relatively obscure web serial on Royal Road back in 2020 has since become one of the biggest success stories in modern fantasy, with millions of copies sold. It's an extraordinary achievement for a series that Dinniman initially began writing for fun, with no real expectations of commercial success.

Much of the books' appeal comes down to Dinniman's unconventional approach to storytelling and characterization, particularly when it comes to Carl himself. For all the absurdity of the intergalactic game show and ridiculous dungeon challenges, Carl is an extremely relatable protagonist. He's just an ordinary guy who's been thrown into an impossible situation, and Dinniman never lets us forget the very human side of a character facing very inhumane circumstances.

Carl's relationship with Donut is arguably the emotional heart of the entire series, balancing laugh-out-loud comedy with moments of genuine heartbreak. And despite the outrageous situations they find themselves in, there's something very grounded about their struggle to survive.

Probably the most impressive thing about Dungeon Crawler Carl, though, is just how easy the books are to devour. With the elaborate leveling systems, character builds, inventory management, complicated mechanics, you'd think reading them would feel like a chore, but somehow, Dinniman makes it all feel effortless. There's something to be said about a series that, by the time you've burned through another 600 pages, you're desperate to read more.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

17 SKI Editor-Approved Gifts For Hardcore Skiers

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

(Photo: Courtesy of DryGuy, Smith, Peak Performance, Hyperice, The North Face, Skida, Heather Hansman, Black Diamond)

Hardcore skiers can be difficult to shop for.

The more involved you become as a skier, the more specific your wants and needs get. Suddenly, the standard goggles no longer apply, and you choose your jacket not based on what looks cool but on what functions in the ways you need. If you have a dedicated skier in your life, it’s not always obvious what to gift them, especially since you’ll want them to use it and love it.

To help ease your mind, we’ve rounded up some of our favorite gift ideas for the people in our lives who eat, sleep, and breathe skiing: from powderhounds to corduroy snobs, this list has a little something for everyone. And we can guarantee it—we’re somewhat of ski hardos ourselves.

We all have that one friend who complains a little too much about being cold. But when your butt freezes the second you sit on the lift, can you blame them? That’s where insulated knickers come in. Throw them on over your base layers for extra protection your derrière didn’t know it needed. Wear them over your base layer for some extra warmth or on their own for a more loose-fitting base under your ski pants. These are the perfect gift for the skier you know who likes to get a lot of days in and stay nice and toasty on the hill. —Giorgia Menetre, Senior Social Media Strategist

Good goggles don’t need to cost a fortune. Dependable click-in, click-out interchangeable lenses are a staple for a reason—they’ll get you through just about every condition.  Smith’s OG reliable goggles offer a wide field of vision, ChromaPop lenses that make the view pop, and a flexible, comfortable frame. Lenses swap easily, with no worry of a magnetic slip-up. Don’t let another ski day go by letting the skier you love squint through bad light. —GM

These booties come with a high price tag, but they’ll be the gift hardcore skiers never forget. This updated compression boot from Normatec takes the pressurized, full-length boot system that has proven recovery patterns and adds heat. Dynamic air circulation throughout the boot’s treatment session helps muscles recover faster and reduces soreness. Remember how good it feels to put an achy and tired calf next to a hot tub jet? Now, imagine that level of recovery from the comfort of your couch. Boots come in three sizes and can fit a wide range of heights.

Please note that boots will ship the week of 11/23. —Jamie Aranoff, SKI Senior Editor

Hestra’s Fall Line mittens are consistently ranked as some of the warmest by our testers. The mittens have a cozy inner layer of brushed polyester and foam to keep hands insulated without overheating. The outside is leather that, once broken in, is soft and dexterous. Wrist straps ensure that the mittens stay with the skier, and not at the bottom of a chairlift. The mittens are on the pricier side, but will surely be a gift that skiers cherish for years. The same warmth also comes in a glove. —JA

Read our full review and browse other warm gloves and mittens here.

Vests are underrated heroes for skiers: they’re a great midlayer for touring and warmer days, easy to layer, and stylish enough to wear around. The Helium Utility down vest from Peak Performance checks all three of these boxes and more. The vest is incredibly lightweight and packs down into its pocket. More importantly, it’s windproof and water-repellent. A cinch at the bottom allows for customization for the right fit. —JA

If you’ve got an impossible-to-shop-for skier on your list, then look no further: this adventure camera from Nocs Provisions (yes, the same brand that makes binoculars) is sure to please. The camera is incredibly easy to use, even with thick gloves or mittens. Its simple one-button technology allows skiers to easily whip it out of a pocket and hit record (it takes both photos and videos). There’s no SD card needed, and content is easy to transfer to either a phone or computer with cords included. The camera takes retro-style photos and videos and comes with a lifetime “no matter what” policy. —JA

For any serious skier, Dermatone chapstick is the ultimate stocking stuffer. Not only will Dermatone protect lips from chapping, but the SPF keeps skin from burning. It also belongs in every first aid kit; it’s proven to help stop frostbite on extremities like lips, noses, and cheeks. Most ski resorts sell Dermatone somewhere in the shop, but here’s your chance to stock up and avoid inflated prices. Your lips, nose, cheeks, and just about everywhere else will thank you. —JA

Fleeces are always a solid gift, but Ridge Merino’s Alpha Wool Insulated jacket goes the extra mile. This fleece is designed to act as an outer layer for warmer days or uphilling, and a midlayer for when temperatures drop. What makes this fleece different is a mix of strategically placed wool to wick away moisture in places you sweat most, matched with a water-resistant Pertex Quantum Air that acts more like a shell than a fleece.  The fleece is complete with a hood, so no matter the weather, you’ll be prepared.—JA

The North Face just inked a deal with U.S. Ski & Snowboard to outfit the men’s and women’s alpine and freestyle teams for the next eight years. Bonus: They just released a version of the kit for the skiing public. The Comp Team Gore-Tex 2L Freeride and 3L Alpine jackets and pants come in alpine and freestyle designs, but the tech is the same: fully wind- and waterproof with plenty of features such as helmet-compatible hoods,  pit and thigh zips, and a multitude of smartly placed pockets. Don’t forget about the U.S. Ski Team crest on the right chest and all of the relevant sponsor badges to complete the look for your U.S. Ski Team superfan. —Samantha Berman, SKI Executive Editor

Here’s the perfect stocking stuffer for the uphiller on your list—or anyone who wants to add a little flair to their kit. Skida’s Pursuit Headband features the new Alpenbloom print, designed by U.S. Ski Teamer Paula Moltzan as part of the brand’s 2026–’27 collaboration with U.S. Ski & Snowboard. Beyond the athlete-designed pattern, the headband is a genuinely useful piece, made with a moisture-wicking, quick-drying fabric that regulates temperature without trapping too much heat and cozy fleece lining for comfort. A secure, nonslip fit keeps it in place whether you’re skiing, skinning, or working up a sweat on a cold-weather run. For guys, check out Ryan Cochran-Siegle’s Under the Lights print, which comes in a hat and a neckwarmer in addition to a headband.—SB

The Black Diamond Spark Mitts have been a tester favorite for years, and for good reason: you can’t go wrong with a short gauntlet mitt with a full leather outer. But the Spark goes beyond boring old performance. Every few seasons, Black Diamond drops a colorway with a fun, embroidered design on the inner side of the index finger. The Angel Collinson mustache model was our longtime favorite, but the current model with shark teeth is just as fun—and a good reminder that skiing isn’t so serious. —Jenny Wiegand, Outside Associate Gear Director

For the skier who skis in all conditions, this is the midlayer to give. At just 9.8 ounces, the Lightstream Full Zip stays unbulky while delivering serious warmth from -10 to 50 degrees. Vivo Extreme insulation is zoned for the job: 60 grams in the body and collar, 40 in the hood and sleeves, and 25 in the underarms and side seams. The 15-denier stretch nylon/PU ripstop shell is tough enough for daily abuse, and it also works as an outer layer for fall bike commutes and dog walks. Pair it with the Lightstream Insulated Short, made for skiers who run cold but won’t give up their shell pants, even on resort days. Full-length side zips let you take the shorts off when things heat up, without removing your ski pants. Light and packable, they’re also the ultimate emergency layer to stash in your boot bag. Available for men and women. —JW

Carv is the perfect gift for the skier who has everything, mostly because it’s the kind of gadget they’d struggle to justify buying for themselves. But if someone gave it to them? Game on. I’ll admit, I was skeptical of this digital ski coach, which uses sensors attached to your boots to analyze your technique and deliver real-time feedback. But after a few seasons, I’m hooked. It turns improving your skiing into a game, and it’s surprisingly addictive. Ted Ligety once told me his secret to topping the Carv leaderboard: find a stretch of groomer where you can make 10 perfect turns, then ski it over and over until you top the leaderboard. Personally, I might get distracted looking for powder, but I’ve found Carv makes even an ordinary groomer day more interesting. It’s fun, genuinely useful, and exactly the sort of gift someone might not know they wanted. Editor’s note: The list price includes a 12-month membership; continued coaching requires renewal. —Sierra Shafer, Editorial Director, Lifestyle

Few things in skiing are more universally useful than a Voilé Strap. These simple, stretchy polyurethane straps are the duct tape of the ski world, minus the sticky residue. Need to secure your skis together? Voilé Strap. Something breaks on your pack? Voilé Strap. Need to attach something to something else in a way the manufacturer probably didn’t intend? You get the idea. Made in Utah, these little problem-solvers come in a variety of lengths and colors and are practically indestructible. Every skier should have a couple tucked into their pack. Or, if you’re like me, scattered around the garage, the gear room, and the back of the car. You never know when you’ll need one, but trust me, you will. —SS

Why do we organize entire lives around sliding down snowy mountains? It’s a fair question, especially when you consider the expense, questionable career decisions, and occasional sleeping arrangements involved in chasing winter. In Powder Days, journalist Heather Hansman sets out to understand the people who’ve made skiing more than a hobby, exploring the history of ski-bum culture and what happens when the mountain towns that made it possible become increasingly difficult places to live. It’s funny, thoughtful, and at times uncomfortable in the best way, asking big questions about access, affordability, and the future of a sport we love. Give it to the skier who plans their life around winter, or anyone who’s ever seriously considered quitting their job to ski more. —SS

I didn’t win a single race in Alta’s town race series last winter, but I did win this boot dryer in the end-of-season raffle, and frankly, I count that as a victory. Wet ski boots aren’t just smelly. Moisture in your liners compromises insulation, making your feet cold even on mild days and potentially cutting a perfectly good ski day short. And if that moisture freezes overnight? Even worse. This compact dryer uses gentle heat and forced air to dry your boots without damaging their liners. What I love most is that it plugs into both a standard outlet and your car’s 12-volt adapter, so you can start drying your boots on the drive home. It’s also small enough to toss in your ski bag for a trip. Not glamorous, but genuinely one of the most useful things I own. —SS

I'm Glad Even Chris Pratt Is Talking Guardians Of The Galaxy Now James Gunn's Schedule Is Wide Open

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

An Internet User Just Made A Comment About James Gunn’s DC Tenure That Makes Me Feel Sad And Weird

Lanterns Showrunner Responds To James Gunn's DC Departure Amid Season 2 Questions

James Gunn’s Removal And Henry Cavill Talking Superman Has Fans Frazzled, But I Have A Hot Take

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Somebody check if Feige still has James Gunn's number.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I thought I'd made peace with saying goodbye to James Gunn's Guardians of the Galaxy. The third movie wrapped things up beautifully, and with Gunn running DC Studios, I figured there wasn't much chance of getting the old gang back together. Well, Hollywood has a funny way of changing plans. Gunn is leaving his leadership position at DC, and Chris Pratt is already talking about the possibility of making another Guardians movie with him. Let me just state for the record that I'm right there with him.

With Avengers: Doomsday approaching on the 2026 movie schedule, Pratt's future as Star-Lord has been on my mind lately. During a recent interview with MovieZine while promoting Way of the Warrior Kid, the actor was asked whether he'd be interested in reuniting with Gunn for a fourth installment. He hadn't spoken to the filmmaker since the DC news broke, but his answer was encouraging:

He directed all three movies, and that's pretty rare… so if he wanted to come back and bring it back, I would love that.

Well, now I want it too. Gunn spent nearly a decade building one of Marvel's strangest superhero teams into something audiences genuinely cared about. Somehow, he also made me cry over a talking raccoon more times than I'd care to admit.

This conversation is considerably less cheerful for DC fans. Gunn and Peter Safran are stepping down as co-heads of DC Studios following the Warner Bros. ownership shake-up, with former Marvel executive Jeremy Latcham taking over Dec. 1, raising plenty of questions about the DC Universe's future.

Gunn isn't exactly sitting at home waiting for Marvel to call. He's still involved with Man of Tomorrow, his follow-up to 2025's Superman, which is scheduled for July 2027. He also has other DC-related work on his plate. Still, stepping away from running an entire studio should eventually give him more freedom to choose what he wants to direct. But does he want to direct a fourth GotG?

Pratt's comments are especially interesting because he previously had some pretty specific thoughts about returning as Star-Lord. Last year, I covered his concerns about finding somebody who could fill Gunn's shoes. At the time, it seemed unlikely the filmmaker would ever be available. Suddenly, that possibility doesn't sound quite so ridiculous.

There's also the matter of where we left Peter Quill. At the end of Guardians of the Galaxy Vol. 3, he returned to Earth to reconnect with his grandfather, while Rocket took charge of a new Guardians lineup. The movie's closing credits promised Star-Lord would return, though the powers that be haven't said in what capacity or which new Marvel flick he might show up in.

Pratt recently confirmed there have been conversations about bringing him back, which is encouraging. Although returning for another Avengers movie and reuniting with Gunn for a fourth Guardians adventure would be two very different things. There's still no indication that Guardians of the Galaxy Vol. 4 is actually happening. Gunn spent years saying the third movie would conclude his trilogy, and he may have no interest in revisiting those characters. I certainly wouldn't blame him for wanting to move on from comic book and superhero fare to something completely different. At this point, he's been helming superhero content for more than a decade. Maybe he wants to spread his wings and take on a different type of movie, which I would be stoked for as well.

Still, I love the idea of Pratt and Gunn finding their way back to each other, especially after everything they accomplished with the first three movies. Maybe it's wishful thinking, but if Gunn ever decides he's ready for another trip into space, I hope Marvel answers the phone.

The first three original Guardians of the Galaxy films are available to stream with a Disney+ subscription.

Ryan graduated from Missouri State University with a BA in English/Creative Writing. An expert in all things horror, Ryan enjoys covering a wide variety of topics. He's also a lifelong comic book fan and an avid watcher of Game of Thrones and House of the Dragon.

NEW YORK, November 16, 2026 – CB Insights today named hellocare.ai to its eighth annual Digital Health 50, showcasing the 50 most promising Digital Health startups in the world.

“AI is beginning to change not just how healthcare organizations operate, but how they actually deliver care,” said Ellen Knapp, Lead Healthcare Analyst at CB Insights. “This year’s Digital Health 50 winners are finding new ways to extend clinicians’ reach, simplify how patients access treatment, and deliver more continuous care for complex conditions.”

“Healthcare is entering a new era where AI, virtual care, and connected hospital room technology can work together to support clinicians and improve the patient experience,” said Labinot Bytyqi, Founder and CEO of hellocare.ai. “Being recognized by CB Insights is an important validation of our vision to make intelligent care delivery available across every hospital room and beyond the hospital walls. We are proud of the health systems trusting us to help bring that vision to life.”

The list primarily includes companies using AI to reshape care delivery, streamline healthcare operations, and improve patients’ access to treatment. Our research team picked winning companies based on CB Insights datasets, including deal activity, industry partnerships, team strength, investor strength, employee headcount, and proprietary metrics.

hellocare.ai provides an integrated intelligent hospital room and virtual care delivery platform designed to help health systems modernize care delivery at scale. Its technology brings AI assisted virtual nursing and rounding, telehealth, virtual patient observation, patient engagement, digital whiteboards, digital door signs, and hospital at home capabilities into a unified ecosystem. By combining bedside technology with AI driven clinical workflows and integration into existing hospital systems, hellocare.ai helps care teams extend their reach, enhance patient safety, and improve operational efficiency. The company works with more than 120 health systems and has supported major enterprise deployments, including a rollout spanning more than 13,000 patient rooms at AdventHealth.

Teledyne LeCroy Introduces Ultra Accelerator Link™ Validation System for AI and HPC Scale-Up Infrastructure

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Xena Z2244 UALink Traffic Generation and Error Injection solution accelerates validation of next-generation AI networks.

MILPITAS, Calif., Nov. 16, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Teledyne LeCroy, a business unit of Teledyne Technologies Incorporated (NYSE: TDY) and a worldwide leader in protocol test solutions, today announced the Xena Z2244 Ultra Accelerator Linkâ¢ (UALinkâ¢) Traffic Generator. Purpose-built for UALink, the system accelerates the development, interoperability testing, and deployment of next-generation AI and high-performance computing (HPC) scale-up infrastructure.

UALink is an open industry standard for AI scale-up fabrics, providing the high-bandwidth, low-latency interconnect required for efficient communication among GPUs, NPUs, and AI accelerators. As AI clusters grow in size and complexity, hyperscalers, cloud service providers, semiconductor companies, and system manufacturers face increasing challenges in validating performance, reliability, interoperability, and deterministic low-latency operation across accelerator fabrics. Built on extensive expertise in high-speed interconnect validation, including industry-leading protocol test solutions for PCI Express and Ethernet, the Xena Z2244 provides comprehensive validation of UALink implementations across 224G and 112G SerDes interfaces, with wire-speed traffic generation of up to 800 Gb/s per device. The platform enables engineering teams to confidently test their designs under realistic traffic conditions, identify performance bottlenecks, verify interoperability, and reduce deployment risk in large-scale AI environments.

"Our work with Teledyne LeCroy reflects Cadence's commitment to advancing the UALink ecosystem," said Arif Khan, Vice President of Product Marketing, Cadence. "The Xena Z2244 brings valuable traffic generation and error injection capabilities to UALink validation, enabling engineering teams to test interoperability, uncover performance bottlenecks and build confidence in their designs. By strengthening the tools available to developers, the Z2244 can help accelerate adoption and expand the market for UALink-based AI infrastructure."

"The Xena Z2244 gives engineering teams a solution to validate UALink scale-up infrastructure with confidence," said Nick Kriczky, Vice President of Products, Compute, Networks and Storage at Teledyne LeCroy. "Our work with Cadence helped accelerate this product's path to market and reinforces our commitment to AI and HPC test and measurement products."

Teledyne LeCroy will jointly demonstrate the Xena Z2244 UALink Traffic Generator in cooperation with Cadence Design Systems at SC26 (Supercomputing 2026), November 17â19 in Chicago, at booth 4303.

The Xena Z2244 UALink Traffic Generator is available for purchase. Contact Teledyne LeCroy for product information, technical specifications, configuration guidance, and regional availability. Learn more about Teledyne LeCroy UALink test solutions at: https://www.teledynelecroy.com/protocolanalyzer/xena-z2244

For additional information about Teledyne LeCroy UALink traffic generation, protocol analysis, error injection, and validation solutions, contact Teledyne LeCroy at +1 (800) 909-7211 or visit: https://www.teledynelecroy.com/protocolanalyzer/xena-z2244

Teledyne LeCroy is a leading manufacturer of advanced oscilloscopes, protocol analyzers, and other test instruments that help engineers verify performance, validate compliance, and debug complex electronic systems quickly and thoroughly. Since 1964, the company has focused on incorporating powerful analysis tools into innovative products that enhance "Time-to-Insight." Faster time to insight helps users identify and resolve defects sooner, improving time to market across a wide range of applications and end markets. Teledyne LeCroy is based in Chestnut Ridge, New York. For more information, visit teledynelecroy.com.

Peptide Tracker Adds Washout Scheduling, Six-Month Schedule Preview, and a Redesigned Pulse in v3.2.0 and v3.3.0

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

New releases let users plan rest periods and dose changes months ahead, see their weight alongside their doses, and share weekly wins with the community

LOS ANGELES, Nov. 16, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Peptide Tracker, the free app for logging and managing peptide protocols, today released versions 3.2.0 and 3.3.0, introducing washout scheduling, a six-month schedule preview, and a redesigned Pulse that lines up a user's weight with the doses behind it.

With the updates, users can build rest periods into any protocol and see every dose day, washout, and titration step on a calendar before they save. Pulse now opens on a single large graph of weight or another metric, with a compound's doses lined up underneath, so users can see what happened in the weeks after a dose went up. In Connect, a new Weekly Wins feature lets users share milestones like "Took my first shot" or "Down a size."

"Our users don't run a protocol one day at a time. They plan cycles, rest periods, and dose changes months out," said Kevin Miller, Co-Founder and CEO of Peptide Tracker. "Now they can see the whole plan before they commit to it, then see how their body actually responded once they're on it."

Since launching in May 2026, Peptide Tracker has surpassed 100,000 downloads and maintains a 4.9-star average rating on the App Store.

Versions 3.2.0 and 3.3.0 are available now on the App Store, Google Play, and the web at peptidetracker.ai.

About Peptide Tracker: Peptide Tracker is a free iOS, Android, and web app that helps people log doses, manage protocols, set reminders, and track their progress over time. Founded in 2026 by Kevin Miller and JP Sio, Peptide Tracker is built by Flat Six Labs. Peptide Tracker is a tracking tool and does not provide medical advice.
